# Backlog Execution Record

The goal remains implementation of the full [backlog](implementation-backlog.md). Completed prerequisites do not narrow the goal or establish “industry-leading” quality by themselves. Only tasks with direct evidence are checked off.

## SmartArt native geometry observations and boundary failures (2026-10-25)

Progress on ADV-02.1.3.1 through [ADR0024](../architecture/decisions/0024-smartart-native-geometry-observation.md), a [pure observation module](../../scripts/research/diagram-geometry-observation.mjs), an optional independent Python extractor and a [24-case producer baseline](../testing/smartart-native-geometry-evidence.json). PowerPoint UI access was actually attempted through Computer Use and rejected because the Mac remains locked. That blocks native interactive editing only; independent layout evidence continued. No unlock workaround, deletion or external publication occurred, and no native UI success is inferred from background accessibility.

The native runner directly converts every generated presentation-mode candidate (three exact layouts × empty/single/standard/long × absent/empty cache) to FODP and PDF in a fresh directory. It verifies input hashes and unchanged sources, explicit frame-name/group association and independent geometry expectations. Every run records native/PDF/picture hashes, raw drawing occurrences/paragraphs/preset/transform/text-area/mirror data, font declarations, PDF text spans/fonts/paint bounds and logs. Geometry resolves only explicit untransformed lengths into page points; raw own/ancestor transforms remain source-only. Group absence/ambiguity, unknown lengths and incomplete geometry are explicit outcomes rather than invented boxes.

Native names can repeat: Cycle arrows share a producer name even though they are separate drawing occurrences. The report retains source association evidence, ambiguity and every source ordinal without assigning semantic ownership by text/order. Captured text, node count and cumulative work are bounded; repeated-name/source-match expansion and sorting charge the budget. Results are detached, ordered and deterministic for the same snapshot. Controlled cache-policy comparison includes raw transformed sizes/positions as well as transform strings, preventing false equality when a rotated arrow changes size. Unknown generated names/ordinals are explicitly excluded from that exact observation comparison.

Seven new portable Node groups cover independent length expectations, frame tolerance/zero, detached results, raw transforms and invalid units, repeated/ambiguous/non-presentation identities, resource amplification/retry, malformed inputs and comparison mutation controls. They are included with the seven content-forest groups in default `test:diagram-research`. Seven optional Python owned-fixture tests additionally cover explicit whitespace/tails/Unicode, text frames, nested lists without copying child drawing text, ancestor transforms, missing/ambiguous groups/master scoping, DTD/byte/whitespace limits and Decimal numeric expectations. Extraction initially missed Process list paragraphs; the new nested-list regression identified and fixed that before the final producer run.

All **24/24** native observations completed in LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0. The corpus has **124** recognized occurrences: **112** axis-aligned boxes independently verified within 1e-8 points and **12** transformed arrows retained raw. All **12** absent/empty-cache control pairs have exact observed geometry equality under the documented policy. Stored drawing text matches the authored markers in all 24 snapshots, while **six long-case PDFs remain unmatched**; empty-case marker matches are vacuous and retain markerCount zero. Process standard/long cases each flag three out-of-frame boxes under both cache policies. [Three picture-specific reviews](../testing/smartart-native-geometry-review.json) confirm Cycle's clipped long top label and Process's right card extending beyond the source frame; standard Cycle labels/arrows are visible. Text retention, PDF extraction, glyph/render fitting, cache validity and native editing remain separate conclusions.

Adversarial review checked five risks: assuming names are unique; treating frame containment as glyph fitting; duplicating/omitting nested native list/frame text; losing ancestor transforms or transformed shape sizes; and allowing repeated-name report amplification beyond work limits. Repeated-source observations, the long clipping negative control, independent text fixtures, transformed-box comparison mutations and budget/retry tests address these within the scoped experiment. Different frame sizes, wider/deeper trees/assistant variants, theme/direction parameters, text-area/font/overflow policy, deterministic family layout and valid/stale cache generation remain incomplete. No task/parent checkbox was closed and the overall implementation objective remains active.

Verification: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and complete `pnpm test` exit **0**. Full tests include diagram research **14** (seven content + seven geometry), timing/event **10**, Core **74**, Parser **549**, Layout **126**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, browser **29**, visual **27**, import integration **61**, release **16**, installed **5**, fixtures/performance **12**, composition **10**, copy **3**, chart **1**, CLI **1** and dev-app **16**, without failures/skips. Optional Python fixtures pass **7/7**. Final source/artifact/whitespace/link audits are recorded separately. Logs: `.tmp/diagram-geometry-{build,typecheck,focused,focused-final,extractor-tests,native-final,full-test,lint}.log`. Native artifact observations and manual image review are not represented as default-CI producer validation.

## Minimum native SmartArt generation and observation correction (2026-10-25)

Progress on ADV-02.2.1.1/.1.2 and the preparation of ADV-02.1.3, through [ADR0023](../architecture/decisions/0023-native-smartart-generation-research.md) and the [reproducible protocol](../testing/smartart-native-generation.md). Standalone pure research writers now serialize ordered content forests, reciprocal parent/sibling transitions and optional exact-layout presentation graphs for process3/orgChart1/cycle2. A fresh package builder uses public Core/Exporter/Parser APIs and explicit offline component snapshots. It owns a separate control label, four diagram relationships/content types and explicit missing/empty cache policies; it is not an arbitrary-source patcher. No production package or public native diagram API is added.

Eleven native research test groups cover identity/typed edges, a single rooted presentation tree, Process descendant-text associations, hierarchy assistants, Cycle's generated closure, storage-order determinism, caller snapshots, invalid topology/scalars/fields, node/depth/work/text/output budgets and clean retry. Independent browser DOM checks now cover all three families × both cache policies × both presentation modes. Three unrelated text-save rounds retain exact diagram components in both modes; source diagram edits remain unbound. Seven pre-existing content-forest groups remain in the suite.

The offline generator independently verifies 24 presentation-mode candidates and 24 content-only negative controls (three layouts × empty/single/standard/long × two caches), including typed identities/topology/text/roles, real component hashes, known layout names and exact no-op. Local borrowed component/source artifacts are not admitted for redistribution. Full schema/semantic certification, managed authoring identities and native family admission remain incomplete.

An important observation error was corrected: the initial statement that all presentation-mode node text was absent was based on word-spacing marker mismatches. Direct and save/reopen PDF extraction actually split words across lines. The observation helper now records raw extracted text, strict word-spacing matches and whitespace-insensitive matches separately, rejects empty/whitespace-only markers and explicitly disclaims distinct occurrence/geometry/editability proof. The original artifacts remain in their unique local directories; current planning no longer repeats the unsupported absence claim.

The plain-text writer also incorrectly set custT, permitting the layout to ignore automatic text formatting. It now omits that flag. A [five-variant Cycle differential](../testing/smartart-native-differential-evidence.json) changes only the customization flag, identity spelling or documented shell/data pairing, then checks direct rendering and native save/reopen separately. The custom-text flag reproduces fragmented words in both stages; all other variants match word spacing. Hashes, command logs, raw extracted text and PNG paths/hashes are retained. Picture inspection of the unlocked reopened Cycle shows three labeled nodes and closing arrows; no calibrated geometry or interactive editability conclusion is drawn.

The [six revised standard presentation cases](../testing/smartart-native-presentation-producer-evidence.json) all save/reopen in the named LibreOfficeDev runtime with retained data inventories, retained native graphic URIs and all word-spacing markers. Cache shape counts remain zero. The [six content-only controls](../testing/smartart-native-candidate-producer-evidence.json) still extract only the ordinary control label, with all authored diagram markers unmatched even under whitespace-insensitive checking. These are separate observations: presentation mapping is necessary in this tested path, and the default content-only specimen remains a negative control. Native producer validation of empty/single/long cases and PowerPoint-generated-candidate UI recognition/edit/re-layout remain untested.

Adversarial review addressed five risks: confusing structural success with native rendering; confusing extraction fragmentation with absent text; locking automatic formatting without authored custom styles; treating a content-only forest as sufficient native output; and losing presentation identities through unrelated saves or browser inspection. Explicit mode boundaries, raw-text/PNG evidence, one-variable controls, exact component preservation and typed graph/browser tests cover these risks within the stated scope. All seven new planning leaves and inherited SmartArt tasks remain unchecked. The whole implementation goal is still incomplete.

Verification: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and complete `pnpm test` exited **0**. Full tests include native import integration **61**, diagram-content research **7**, timing/event **10**, Core **74**, Parser **549**, Layout **126**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, browser **29**, visual **27**, release **16**, installed **5**, fixtures/performance **12**, composition **10**, copy **3**, chart **1**, CLI **1** and dev-app **16**, without failures/skips. After extending presentation-mode preservation/DOM and whitespace-only-marker coverage, final focused verification passes **18/18** and the complete import integration rerun passes **61/61**; final lint and documentation/type examples pass. Artifact audits verify all 48 candidate hashes, ten differential picture hashes, six positive and six negative producer observations, script syntax and 353 unique hierarchical task IDs. Logs: `.tmp/native-diagram-revised-{build,typecheck,lint,lint-final,full-test,focused-final,import-final,generated,producer,bare-generated,bare-producer,differential}.log`. Native differential and producer runners terminate successfully; observation matches are not task-completion claims.

- **REL-06:** [Shared corpus](../testing/fixture-corpus.md) adds seven deterministic fixtures, actual PNG data, six pinned-font snapshots, generated PPTX/IR/SVG artifacts, XML parseability/relationship/content-type checks and negative verifier tests.
- **REL-03:** [Performance runner and recorded baseline](../performance/README.md) measure 10/50/200-slide mixed decks in separate processes, with two warmups and five measured samples, raw variance and output/memory evidence.
- **REL-02, partial:** Seven fixtures processed by native macOS LibreOfficeDev, saved as PPTX, reopened and exported as PDF. [Evidence](../testing/libreoffice-evidence.json) includes input/saved/PDF hashes, XML/CRC checks and native chart/workbook counts. Interactive edits, visual fidelity, stable LibreOffice and other platforms remain unverified. PowerPoint `16.104` is installed but not yet exercised.

## Production regressions found and fixed

1. Overlapping exports with asynchronous image loads shared a module-global chart counter. The new regression reproduced non-identical archives; chart IDs now use an invocation-local allocator. Package-local and corpus tests compare eight concurrent exports to the isolated result.
2. Hyperlink query strings were interpolated unescaped into relationship XML. The DOM-parser corpus test rejected a URL containing `&`. Relationship attributes now use the existing XML encoder; focused and package tests cover the regression.

## Verification and adversarial review

`pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the full `pnpm test` suite passed after these code changes. The full suite includes 10 Chromium visual cases, browser-bundle consumers, chart parity, package tests and the 12 corpus/statistics subtests. All six new snapshots were inspected; the connector fixture was adjusted to explicitly request a visible stroke rather than accepting an invisible default.

- **Compatibility:** No public signatures or authoring defaults changed. Export archive numbering is deterministic per invocation; XML-decoded link targets retain their original values.
- **Hidden loss:** Tests inspect all internal relationships/content types and embedded workbooks. LibreOffice reopen success is recorded separately and does not assert lossless round-trip.
- **Layout divergence:** Corpus includes rotation, flipped nested groups, merged cells and image crop. Pinned-browser snapshots are bounded evidence; measured typography and native application parity remain open.
- **Failure behavior:** A missing-path fixture asserts explicit warnings, and deliberate XML/relationship/content-type corruption proves the verifier catches those failures.
- **Architecture:** No runtime dependency or package was added. Fixtures/tooling use existing package entry points; test-only archive/XML inspection does not become a production parser. Chart identity stays inside each exporter invocation.

The script rejects non-increasing/mixed versions, ambiguous registry failures, and missing release notes before changing manifests. Dry run, cancellation and all validation occur before version changes; publication failure retains target versions with a recovery path.

Browser execution blocks network requests; npm installation may resolve the declared fflate dependency.
- Every package now declares the already documented Node >=20 installation/tooling requirement. No runtime dependency was added, no package version was changed, and no npm publication was performed.

Current-version tarball testing is explicitly distinguished from exact target-version artifact verification.

It consumes a caller-supplied live document, loaded FontFace inventory, synchronous glyph/style coverage checks and ordered optional fallback families. No implicit font loading, system-family fallback, filesystem access, network access, new package or runtime dependency was added.

Native chart label flow and paint-effect bounds remain explicitly unchecked; browser analysis is not native PowerPoint fidelity evidence.
- Twelve [browser measurement cases](../../packages/svg-renderer/test/measurement.spec.mjs) cover actual Latin advances against independent Canvas metrics, mixed Latin/CJK, explicit repeated/trailing breaks, empty paragraphs, zero-width/no-wrap, lists/hanging/alignment, margins/shrink/spacing, font removal/renaming, unknown/unloaded fonts, asynchronous coverage errors, literal text, hostile host CSS, disposal, hidden/transformed documents and repeatability. Async coverage cannot silently pass or leak unhandled rejections. Font loading remains caller-owned; per-call DOM nodes are cleaned up and disposal is idempotent. ## Fixed-size composition delivered

- **LAY-04 completed:** Public `LayoutOptions.composition` and typed align/distribute/stack rules provide explicit selection order, horizontal/vertical directions, padded areas, fixed minimum gaps, start/center/end/space-between packing and cross-axis alignment. Frame or rotated/flipped visual bounds can drive placement. [Contract and checked example](../api/composition.md).
- Composition validates options before execution and runs on a private normalized clone. Each rule either applies all valid placements or emits an actionable warning and leaves its geometry unchanged; earlier rules remain effective. Hidden content is opt-in, drawing order is stable, and group operations use local coordinateSize. No Core/IR state, dependency or package was added.
- Placement precedes connector/image/chart resolution. Anchored connectors follow arranged shapes; independently selecting them is diagnosed. Free connectors translate all endpoints/routes using actual path bounds, even if an authored box differs. Image fitting and chart geometry use the composed allocation. Overflow analysis runs afterward.
- Nineteen [unit tests](../../packages/layout/test/composition.test.mjs) exercise all main align/packing modes, missing IDs, malformed options, empty/single selections, hidden content, templates, nested groups, rotation, extreme numeric translations and repeatable/non-mutating behavior. A heterogeneous-dimension loop checks 40 additional direction/sample combinations against gap, size and centering invariants.
- Two [cross-format tests](../../scripts/composition-parity.test.mjs) compare a mixed shape/image/table/chart/group/connector deck against independently positioned authoring. Resolved geometry, complete PPTX bytes and SVG agree; a failed rule preserves valid output while forwarding identical diagnostics. `pnpm test:composition` is part of the full suite. Installed TypeScript consumers exercise the new public options through Layout, export and preview.
- Verification: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm docs:check` and the full `pnpm test` suite passed. The local suite log is `.tmp/goal-composition-tests.log`; existing 22 Chromium cases and isolated npm consumers also pass.
- Adversarial review covered partial-rule application, accidental drawing-order changes, connector/reference drift, nested coordinate confusion and numeric edge cases. Preflight validation prevents partial connector translation when deltas exceed finite range; a machine-rounding tolerance avoids rejecting exact decimal/rotated fits. Existing default calls and IR remain compatible. No automatic sizing, inferred routing, grid constraints or pagination is claimed.
- The full backlog remains the goal. All other unchecked tasks require their own implementation and verification; this milestone does not establish complete Office parity or completion of the goal.


## Measured table rows delivered

- **LAY-07 completed:** [Explicit table sizing](../api/table-sizing.md) measures rich-text cell content at fixed column/span widths and applies positive min/max automatic row bounds. Unlisted rows remain fixed. Shorter span constraints resolve first, followed by deterministic equal growth across eligible rows with cap redistribution. This balanced policy does not claim globally minimum table height.
- `preserve` rejects an overflowing table's sizing atomically; `clip` applies bounded heights with warnings. Failed providers, invalid grid/targets, width mismatches and nonfinite totals always retain authored geometry. Sizing runs before composition and connectors, and does not mutate authoring or supplied IR. Identical sizing/final-analysis diagnostics are deduplicated.
- The shared cell-grid resolver now serves both sizing and table overflow analysis. Successful `LayoutTableElement.cellLayout` records row/cell/column indices and table-local boxes. SVG uses positioned XHTML cells with table/row/cell semantics and explicit clipping; legacy tables keep their previous rendering path. PPTX keeps native editable tables and emits the same resolved row/frame dimensions.
- Fifteen [Layout tests](../../packages/layout/test/table-sizing.test.mjs) cover spans, overlapping constraints, rich runs, margins, fixed rows/minima/maxima, rollback/clip, hidden/nested content, missing providers, invalid grids, overflowed numeric totals, source detachment and configurable tolerance. Two [browser/export tests](../../packages/svg-renderer/test/measurement.spec.mjs) use the pinned font provider, inspect real DOM rectangles and text extents, and extract row/frame heights from the generated PPTX. They prove both fitting and intentionally clipped cases.
- Verification found a real browser quantization boundary: a 36-point content area produced a 36.015625-point text rectangle. Table tolerance is explicit, defaults to 0.02 points, and has a zero-tolerance regression. The real-font merged fixture resolves to 47/47-point rows; capped clip mode resolves to 20/20 and verifies overflowing text remains clipped without changing cell bounds.
- Full `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` passed, including 24 Chromium cases, package/corpus tests, isolated npm consumers and existing composition parity checks. Targeted browser checks were rerun after adding text-extent assertions. Local suite record: `.tmp/goal-table-sizing-tests.log`. Public types and the [checked example](../examples/table-sizing.ts) are covered by documentation/consumer compilation.
- Adversarial review checked browser minimum-content row expansion, merged-cell allocation drift, partial updates on measurement failure, hidden/nested coordinate ownership and subpixel/numeric overflow. Columns remain fixed; native Office reflow and border/style fidelity remain separate evidence/work. No pagination or universal Office row-height enforcement is claimed.
- All remaining backlog tasks stay active and unchecked until their own implementation and evidence are complete.

## Intrinsic sizing LAY-06

Delivered explicit `LayoutOptions.intrinsicSizing` without Core/IR schema changes. Selected axes derive from content or parent-local dimensions; omitted axes retain normalized sizes. Text uses injected measurement, images require metadata and preserve content-derived aspect ratios, and groups rebase negative child coordinates while preserving scale and world positions under rotation/flips. Cycles and unavailable/unsatisfiable inputs return actionable warnings and discard the entire intrinsic stage. Table measurement precedes fitting; composition and final connector/image/chart resolution follow it.

Evidence:

- [17 focused Layout cases](../../packages/layout/test/intrinsic.test.mjs): fixed-axis/input preservation, deterministic repeat resolution, width-before-height/margins, bounds/atomic failure, image ratios/missing metadata, 16 rotation/flip combinations, local parent dimensions, dependency cycles in both orders, connector bounds, hidden inputs, nested order independence, mixed parent/content axes, provider/numeric failure, table integration and downstream composition.
- [Two cross-package cases](../../scripts/intrinsic-parity.test.mjs): complete PPTX bytes and SVG equal independently sized authoring; failed sizing retains original output and forwards matching warnings. Included in `test:composition` and the full root suite.
- [Pinned-font Chromium case](../../packages/svg-renderer/test/measurement.spec.mjs): natural width agrees with Canvas advances, constrained width changes measured height, and PPTX frame extents match. Browser coverage now totals 25 cases including existing visual snapshots.
- [API contract and checked example](../api/intrinsic-sizing.md), architecture stage ordering, package docs and isolated installed TypeScript consumers updated.

Validation: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, full `pnpm test` and `git diff --check` passed. Layout has 76 passing tests; the full suite also covers 25 Chromium cases, installed consumers, browser bundles, chart parity, fixture/performance checks and four composition/intrinsic parity cases. Local run logs are `.tmp/goal-intrinsic-{build,typecheck,lint,tests}.log`.

Adversarial review:

1. **False auto provenance:** IR does not retain authored/omitted size provenance. Explicit axis rules opt into changes; tests verify untouched axes and source documents, rather than inferring auto from zero.
2. **Group drift/order dependence:** independent trigonometric corner checks cover flips/rotation/nesting; reverse rule ordering and mixed parent/content axes match reference geometry. Original zero frame axes use documented seed scale 1.
3. **Partial failure:** max conflicts, missing targets/providers, numeric overflow and cycles retain the pre-intrinsic geometry; integration tests verify identical retained export bytes and preview.
4. **Unproven typography:** the real-font test validates frame calculation and exported geometry. Shared line layout and native Office reflow remain unverified; docs explicitly distinguish them.
5. **Hidden pipeline coupling:** table fitting is covered, but later child composition can invalidate a fitted group's enclosure. Ordering and this limitation are explicit; no iterative solver or hidden I/O was introduced. Shared internal connector geometry avoids two divergent implementations.

LAY-06 and child tasks LAY-06.1/06.2 are complete for their documented contract. The full implementation goal remains open; grid/pagination, import/save, advanced capabilities and the remaining backlog are not implied complete.

## Grid tracks LAY-05.1

Delivered `GridComposition`, `GridTrack` and `GridPlacement` through public Layout exports. Explicit fixed/content/fraction rows and columns support min/max, positive fraction weights, gaps, spans, frame/visual bounds and per-cell start/center/end alignment. Placement keeps element sizes and z-order intact, uses resolved intrinsic frames, and precedes connector resolution. Hidden items do not contribute demands; explicit tracks remain. Malformed rules throw before execution; infeasible rules produce `composition-grid-unsatisfiable` and retain all positions for that rule.

The pure internal interval solver processes demands by ending track and fills rightmost capacity first. This avoids false overflow for overlapping spans that can share capacity. Weighted extra space is redistributed after caps; finite weights are normalized to avoid overflowing their sum. The documented allocation policy is deliberately explicit rather than claiming CSS Grid semantics.

Evidence:

- [11 focused tests](../../packages/layout/test/grid.test.mjs), including 14,175 integer feasibility comparisons against an independent exhaustive assignment oracle, mixed tracks, caps, extreme weights, span gaps, invalid contracts, failure atomicity, visual/group coordinates, hidden items, overlap/order and intrinsic → grid → anchored connector integration.
- [Cross-package parity](../../scripts/composition-parity.test.mjs) compares complete generated PPTX bytes and SVG with independently authored positions, including a connector following the arranged elements.
- [API and checked example](../api/grid.md), architecture boundaries and installed TypeScript consumer checks cover the public contract. No package/dependency or Core/IR schema was added.

`pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and full `pnpm test` passed. The final additional intrinsic/grid integration test passed with all Layout tests rerun (87 total). Full regression includes 25 Chromium cases, installed packages, browser bundles, fixture/benchmark checks, chart parity and five composition/intrinsic/grid parity cases. Local logs: `.tmp/goal-grid-{build,typecheck,lint,tests,final-layout}.log`.

Adversarial review:

1. **False infeasibility:** overlapping intervals can share capacity; exhaustive feasible assignments independently confirm the solver on the bounded domain, including fixed zero capacities and unsatisfiable cases.
2. **Order dependence:** track demands use geometric ordering, not selection order. Reversed placements yield identical output and preserve drawing order.
3. **Hidden resizing:** grids move fixed frames only. Width-driven text reflow/stretch is explicitly excluded; intrinsic sizing precedes placement and has a dedicated integration case.
4. **Partial/overflow failures:** fixed/capped conflicts reject the whole rule; later rules still run. Huge finite weights are normalized and tested.
5. **Boundary drift:** nested group-local placement and visual rotation have independent expected coordinates; generated PPTX/SVG equal manual authoring. No native Office layout parity is inferred.

LAY-05.1 is complete. LAY-05 and LAY-05.2 remain open: general bounded size/position constraints, reference dependencies and their cycle/conflict semantics still require implementation. This does not reduce the full backlog goal.

## Frame constraints LAY-05.2

Delivered explicit bounded affine size/position constraints as a composition operation. Public contracts include `ConstraintsComposition`, `ConstrainedElement`, `FrameConstraint` and affine term/metric types. Each selected x/y/width/height property is one dependency node; right/bottom/center metrics expand into frame component dependencies. An iterative topological queue resolves references independent of element declaration order, and reports an actual cycle chain without recursion. Equalities must satisfy bounds; bounds-only properties clamp incoming values. Geometry remains detached until all checks pass.

Constraints use the existing composition ownership and rule-order contract. Table/intrinsic sizing precedes them; later grids/stacks can consume changed sizes, while final connectors/images/charts consume final frames. Tables and free connectors translate but cannot silently resize their structural geometry. Anchored connectors cannot supply unresolved geometry as references. Groups resize their frame while retaining local coordinateSize, intentionally scaling children.

Evidence:

- [18 focused cases](../../packages/layout/test/constraints.test.mjs): reversed dependency declarations, multi-term centering, bounds/equality conflicts, bound references, stable cycle chains, zero factors, nonfinite arithmetic, missing references, hidden semantics, group scaling, anchored/free connectors, table atomicity, rule precedence, a 12,000-node chain, grid integration and 100 seeded branching DAGs with independent forward evaluation.
- [Two cross-package cases](../../scripts/constraints-parity.test.mjs): complete PPTX bytes/SVG equal independently authored shape/image/chart/group geometry with connectors; cycles retain original bytes/slides and forward identical actionable diagnostics. Included in the full root test command.
- [API contract and checked example](../api/constraints.md), architecture ownership, capability matrix, changelog and isolated installed TypeScript consumers updated.

Validation: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, full `pnpm test`, and `git diff --check` passed. Layout has 105 passing cases; full regression includes 25 Chromium cases, installed consumers, browser bundles, corpus/benchmarks, chart parity and seven composition/grid/intrinsic/constraint parity cases. Local logs: `.tmp/goal-constraints-{build,typecheck,lint,tests}.log`.

Adversarial review:

1. **Stale references:** expressions read same-rule resolved properties, not declaration-order snapshots. Anchored connector geometry is explicitly rejected before final resolution; tests cover target updates and rejection.
2. **Partial failure:** a valid independent property can resolve before a cycle/conflict is found. All candidates remain private until completion, and tests assert the whole rule retains incoming geometry while prior/later rules keep their documented behavior.
3. **Invalid structural resize:** table dimensions must remain consistent with rows/columns and free connector frames with points. Both reject unsupported dimension changes instead of generating inconsistent output.
4. **Depth and numeric failure:** a 12,000-node chain proves iterative dependency handling; 100 seeded branching DAGs independently verify negative factors and multi-term values; overflowing values/extents diagnose rather than commit.
5. **Overstated scope:** documented constraints are directed affine relationships. Every cycle, even an algebraically redundant one, is rejected. They do not implement simultaneous equation optimization, automatic text reflow or CSS layout. Geometry/output tests do not assert native Office typography parity.

LAY-05.2 and parent LAY-05 are complete for this explicit contract. LAY-08 pagination and all remaining import/round-trip/authoring/advanced/ecosystem tasks remain part of the active implementation goal.

## Table pagination LAY-08

Delivered explicit `LayoutOptions.tablePagination` and `LayoutResult.pagination.fragments`. A pure row-block planner splits top-level tables only at closed merge boundaries, repeats selected leading rows and respects separate first/continuation areas. Default oversized-block behavior retains the source; explicit overflow places the indivisible block alone and warns. The per-table commit preserves original first-fragment IDs, allocates deterministic collision-checked continuation identities, inserts pages, and records source row indices and repeated-header count. Continuations use the selected layout and metadata, not cloned source siblings/notes.

Evidence:

- [18 Layout tests](../../packages/layout/test/pagination.test.mjs): row conservation, repeated resolution and detachment; metadata/notes/links; header/body merge boundaries and transitive merged blocks; oversized preserve/overflow; continuation layouts; IDs and budgets; same-slide conflicts; hidden and malformed targets; header-only/zero-header tables; measured row integration; final connector bounds; independent-source ordering; invalid/nonfinite grids; bounded varied row/block plans.
- [Three cross-package cases](../../scripts/pagination-parity.test.mjs): complete PPTX bytes and exact-cell SVG match independently split decks; inserted pages preserve final hyperlink destinations; default failure retains bytes/slides and forwards diagnostics alongside existing SVG action warnings. An additional merged-header/body fixture matches manual PPTX output and asserts rowSpan/vMerge markers and row heights on both pages.
- [Real-browser geometry](../../packages/svg-renderer/test/measurement.spec.mjs): all generated pages show the expected repeated header/body text, actual cell rectangles and matching exported row heights. Browser coverage totals 26 cases.
- [API/checked example](../api/table-pagination.md), architecture, changelog, installed TypeScript consumers and source-map types updated.

Validation: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, full `pnpm test` and `git diff --check` passed. Full Layout coverage is 123 cases. The complete suite passed with 26 Chromium cases and nine composition-family parity cases; the subsequently added merged native-table fixture passed in the three-case pagination suite. Final composition-family parity rerun covers all ten cases. Logs are `.tmp/goal-pagination-{full-build,typecheck,lint,tests,parity,browser}.log`.

Adversarial review:

1. **Lost/duplicated data:** source-row maps and bounded row conservation tests ensure only headers repeat. All candidate pages are planned before commit, so later oversized/limit/collision failures cannot leave a truncated table.
2. **Broken merges:** crossing header boundaries rejects; transitively overlapping body spans remain a single block. Unit tests validate each fragment grid, and native-package tests verify merged header/body markers against manual authoring.
3. **Identity and links:** generated identities reserve the full document namespace, and repeated calls leave source state unchanged. Native relationships target final slide indices; source jumps still mean the original first fragment. Dynamic page fields remain AUT-08, not an implicit pagination feature.
4. **Preview/export divergence:** successful fragments attach exact cell geometry; browser DOM rectangles and native row dimensions match. Text fit still requires measurement and native Office font reflow is not asserted.
5. **Scope and allocation:** one top-level unrotated table per source slide is explicit; unsupported cases diagnose. Continuations copy only selected rows and metadata, avoiding per-page clones of the entire source table/slide. Generic story flow and multi-table scheduling remain separate capabilities.

LAY-08, LAY-08.1 and LAY-08.2 are complete for this explicit table continuation contract. LAY-01–08 now have implementation/verification evidence. Remaining reliability application checks, richer authoring, import/round-trip, advanced capabilities and ecosystem work remain in the full active goal.

## Import/save contract IMP-01 — design recorded, runtime pending

[ADR 0001](../architecture/decisions/0001-import-preservation-save.md) records Parser-owned source/session/save state, the one-way public Exporter dependency for explicit rebuild, and the exclusion of XML/ZIP/opaque handles from Core/IR. It defines mandatory no-op/edit/rebuild modes, byte snapshotting, operation-specific capability findings, prepared verified output, exact per-plan loss consent, stale-snapshot checks, disposal/cancellation/retry behavior and implementation gates.

The [compile-only protocol consumer](../examples/import-save-contract.ts) checks readonly session identity and nested reports, nonconstructible save plans, explicit modes and named loss consent. It imports only the existing Core public entry. No parser runtime, package export, ZIP inspection or round-trip functionality is claimed. IMP-01 and children remain unchecked until actual public Node/browser session tests demonstrate their runtime requirements. This records a concrete architectural prerequisite for the upcoming inspector rather than marking a type declaration as a shipped feature.

Adversarial design review addressed five risks: source-state leakage into Core; parser/exporter dependency cycles; stale or cross-session plans; unknown closure loss hidden behind generic consent; and partial/cancelled output being mistaken for success. Each has a named runtime conformance gate in the ADR. The current evidence proves the contract/type proposal only, not those future implementations.

Validation for this contract-only slice: `pnpm docs:check` passed (local links, contract checks and all TypeScript examples, including negative consumers), and `git diff --check` passed. Runtime code was not changed in this slice; no parser runtime or full runtime-suite result is implied.

## Bounded archive foundation — IMP-02 remains partial

The new experimental `@pptkit/pptx-parser` package exposes `inspectZipArchive` through its root, browser ESM and global entries. It owns ZIP32 inventory, input snapshots, local/central consistency, descriptors, path aliases and hierarchy checks, resource budgets, CRC verification and cooperative cancellation. See the [public contract](../api/archive-inspection.md). It depends only on the already-used fflate library; Core, Layout and the existing format writers do not import parser state. Installed-tarball consumers and package boundary checks include the new package.

An independent RFC 1951 structural scanner checks stored/fixed/dynamic blocks, Huffman alphabets, repeats, final-block termination, reserved symbols and backward references before decoding. It counts expanded output without allocating it, enforcing entry/total/ratio budgets even when ZIP sizes lie. Whole bytes after the final block are rejected; unused final-byte padding bits remain allowed. CRC and actual decoded counts provide a separate payload check. The scanner and decoder yield cooperatively, including streams of empty blocks.

Verification includes 25 parser tests: independent ZIP record construction; bit-level malformed DEFLATE fixtures; all truncation prefixes for sample archives/streams; declared and actual budget violations; 80 seeded cross-decoder cases against Node zlib across levels/strategies/window sizes; full 32 KiB history across blocks; deep paths; input ownership; and cancellation identity. Seven real PPTKit fixture decks are inspected against an independent ZIP reader. Installed Node, TypeScript, browser ESM and browser global consumers cover public-only use.

Adversarial review addressed five concrete failure modes:

1. **Hidden compressed content:** CRC/size checks alone accepted the risk of trailing data inside a declared compressed span. Independent terminal-block validation now rejects whole trailing bytes and concatenated streams; padding has a positive fixture.
2. **Forged size / history:** declared lengths cannot authorize unbounded inflation or invalid back-references. Preflight output counting and actual decoder budgets both apply; bomb, reserved-code and history fixtures exercise rejection.
3. **Path ambiguity / complexity:** percent/NFC/case aliases, file/directory conflicts and special filesystem records reject. Slash-terminated canonical keys are sorted for adjacent-prefix checks, avoiding quadratic ancestor construction for deep paths; 16,000-segment and near-sibling cases verify behavior.
4. **Cancellation / source identity:** copied bytes and captured limits/signal prevent caller mutation from changing in-flight interpretation. Both nonempty and empty-block streams have asynchronous cancellation tests; no partial inventory is returned.
5. **False import claims:** this API only inspects archive structure. XML grammar/limits, content types, relationships, Strict/Transitional handling, semantic import, preservation sessions and producer-specific application evidence remain open. IMP-01/IMP-02 and their children remain unchecked.

Final-state validation: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, and `pnpm test` all passed after the deep-path improvement and all 25 parser cases were in place. The full suite includes 26 browser visual/measurement tests, 5 installed-package consumer checks, 12 fixture/benchmark cases, 3 browser-bundle cases, chart parity, 10 composition/intrinsic/constraint/pagination parity cases, and the existing package/dev-app suites. `git diff --check` also passed. This supersedes the earlier lint failure caused by the missing new-package smoke test; that smoke test now exercises real public inspection and truncation rejection.

## Bounded XML parts — IMP-02 remains partial

`@pptkit/pptx-parser` now exposes `inspectXmlPart`, XML limits/result types and `DEFAULT_XML_LIMITS` from root/browser/global entries. The [contract](../api/xml-inspection.md) defines XML 1.0 namespace-aware well-formedness, UTF-8 and BOM-marked UTF-16 decoding, DTD rejection, source/node/depth/attribute/content/pending-span budgets, cancellation and located errors. Source bytes/options are captured before asynchronous parsing. The public result is inventory; the private optional ordered tree is not a preservation serializer.

Pinned saxes 6.0.0 owns grammar checking behind the adapter. This dependency was already in the workspace's development graph; the Parser now declares it directly. Its upstream is archived, so wrapper regressions and independent Chromium comparisons are part of the delivery evidence. The browser build uses a CommonJS conversion plugin only for Parser, bundles required XML code, and includes source license notices in both standalone bundles and the packed package. No Core/Layout/Exporter dependency direction changes.

Verification adds 18 XML tests (43 total Parser tests) covering malformed bytes/structure, namespace aliases/rebinding, normalization, DTD/external-entity rejection, all budget boundaries, incomplete long tokens, chunk-split Unicode/entities, repeated BOMs, detached input/signal identity, locations and concurrent failure/cancellation isolation. Seven generated PPTX fixture decks and embedded workbook XML are inspected; XML root/namespace/element/attribute inventories are compared with independent Chromium DOMParser results. Browser tests additionally compare accepted/rejected well-formedness cases, enforce limits and observe zero resource requests. Installed Node/TypeScript/browser ESM/global consumers exercise the public XML API.

[Native XML evidence](../testing/xml-inspection-evidence.json) records 128 XML/relationship parts across the seven retained LibreOfficeDev-produced PPTX artifacts. Each source hash matches the earlier native application record. `pnpm inspect:native-xml` runs the public ZIP inspector and uses Python's independent ZIP reader over the same immutable byte snapshot before public XML inspection. It writes a report only after every fixture passes. This is additional structure evidence against real producer output, not a new application open/edit/save run or semantic round-trip proof.

Adversarial review addressed five concrete risks:

1. **Unbounded unfinished tokens:** source chunks and a pending-event span budget stop long attributes/comments/CDATA/PI/DOCTYPE/text before a closing delimiter is required. Completed-token callbacks also enforce the span, preventing a final delimiter from bypassing the limit. Tests include malformed and valid over-limit cases.
2. **False budget failures:** the first boundary test exposed saxes 6's post-write position double-counting. Event-time cursors remain usable; post-write checks use the wrapper's own decoded count. A many-short-tags test spans multiple chunks under a four-character pending budget.
3. **Encoding ambiguity:** fatal decoding rejects invalid sequences/surrogates and inconsistent declarations. Repeated BOMs are explicitly rejected so decoder and parser BOM handling cannot jointly accept two prolog BOMs. UTF-8 and both UTF-16 byte orders have positive and negative cases.
4. **Hidden external work / corrupt partial success:** DTDs and custom entities reject; comments/PI/CDATA remain inert. The first parse error throws, close must succeed before return, cancellation yields no inventory, and concurrent parser instances cannot share namespace/failure state.
5. **Overclaiming fidelity:** well-formed XML and a private tree are not schema validity, OPC validity, Strict/Transitional interpretation or source-preserving serialization. Package-wide aggregate XML budgets, content-type dispatch, relationship graph validation, dialect/AlternateContent handling, semantic import and session conformance remain open. No IMP task is marked complete for this substrate alone.

The complete workspace `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` chain passed with the final runtime implementation, including 42 Parser cases, 26 visual/measurement cases, 4 browser-bundle cases, installed consumers and all fixture/parity suites. A final concurrency-only test and native-evidence command were then added. The final `pnpm --filter @pptkit/pptx-parser test` run passed all 43 cases; `pnpm inspect:native-xml` passed 7 fixtures / 128 XML parts; final `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` passed. The earlier XML boundary-test failures were resolved in the adapter, not hidden by raising the production budget.

## OPC envelope and relationship graph — IMP-02 remains partial

`inspectOpcPackage` now composes ZIP inspection, private payload reading, XML parsing, logical part-name mapping, content types and internal/external relationship inventories. The [public profile](../api/opc-inspection.md) distinguishes this graph validation from presentation-specific schema validity or semantic import. Defaults/overrides, relationship name/type/source agreement, scoped IDs, missing/forbidden targets, fragments, shared/cyclic references and explicit unsupported physical/MCE cases have focused coverage.

Package-wide XML limits are enforced during parsing through a private metric observer; they do not wait for the entire XML tree to complete. The content-types stream counts toward aggregate XML budgets but is not an OPC part. Binary parts are ZIP-verified and not recursively interpreted. Relationship records have a separate aggregate count. Input bytes, all limit groups and signal identity are captured before yielding; source/target lookup uses ASCII-case identity and no hidden URL or filesystem resolution.

The implementation adds no dependency and keeps all format policy in Parser. Only the private XML reader gains metric observation and declaration provenance needed by the package layer. OPC-defined XML uses its narrower encoding-declaration rule, while the generic XML inspector retains its existing documented profile. Public callers receive detached metadata, never payload maps or mutable library trees.

Verification now includes 20 OPC cases (63 Parser tests total), with a 600-node cyclic graph, source-local Unicode IDs, encoded Unicode/space paths, override precedence, malformed declarations/targets, external references without fetching, all aggregate budget boundaries, ordering independence, ownership/cancellation and OPC-specific UTF-16 declaration tests. Generated PPTX fixtures and embedded chart workbook packages are checked alongside the existing independent Chromium verifier. Browser and installed Node/TypeScript/ESM/global tests exercise graph results and failure behavior through public entries.

[Re-inspected native evidence](../testing/xml-inspection-evidence.json) records 123 logical parts, 111 relationships and 128 XML/relationship streams across seven hash-matched LibreOfficeDev-produced artifacts. `pnpm inspect:native-xml` reproduces the check using public ZIP/OPC/XML APIs and independent Python ZIP extraction of the immutable source snapshot. It does not rerun Office or establish visual/editing/round-trip fidelity.

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **Reference confusion:** targets resolve from the source part, not `_rels`; logical and physical names are distinct; non-ASCII case is not folded; reserved relationship endpoints and missing targets reject. Shared resources, fragment-only source links and cycles remain legal.
2. **Incomplete content-type coverage:** defaults/overrides use the correct identity and precedence; every ordinary part has a type; unreferenced XML and extensionless XML are checked by media type. Relationship parts have their implicit OPC type and reject conflicting declarations/parameters.
3. **Aggregate-limit bypass:** many individually valid XML parts can exceed the package budget. Shared character/node/content counters reject while parsing; the types stream is included. A large unfinished token demonstrates rejection before full parse completion.
4. **Hidden extension/resource work:** external targets are retained without resolving or fetching. Relationship MCE extensions, interleaved pieces and trash items reject explicitly; unsupported structure is not silently discarded under a success result. Basic external lexical screening is not a URL permission policy.
5. **Schema/profile overclaim:** reverse review found OPC control XML's encoding declaration rules are narrower than generic XML. Positive UTF-16 and negative UTF-16LE/BE declaration cases now distinguish the layers. Presentation/dialect interpretation, source XML relationship-ID references, full MCE/container policies and preservation sessions remain open; IMP-01/IMP-02 are not closed by this envelope slice.

Final-state logs confirm that the complete `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test`, and `pnpm inspect:native-xml` chain passed after encoding hardening and the 600-node cyclic-graph case. The Parser suite passed all 63 cases; native inspection passed 7 fixtures / 128 XML streams. The process is terminal; this evidence supersedes the first-pass results.


## PresentationML entry identification — IMP-02.3 remains partial

The experimental public `inspectPresentationPackage` resolves the package-level officeDocument relationship, verifies the target main type and matching PresentationML root namespace, and reports entry dialect, document kind, macro-enabled type and suggested extension alongside the OPC inventory. It recognizes six presentation/template/slideshow main content types and both Strict/Transitional entry identifiers. See the [public contract](../api/presentation-inspection.md) and [checked example](../examples/presentation-inspection.ts).

Parser owns this layer above OPC. It uses one bounded read, no new dependency, no Core state and no filesystem/network calls. Existing lower-layer limits, snapshots and cancellation apply. Classification errors have public presentation-invalid/presentation-unsupported codes and source-part labels. Main-part names are resolved through relationships; conventional ZIP paths and filename extensions are not heuristics.

Eight new focused tests bring Parser to 71 cases. They cover twelve type/dialect combinations, namespace aliases/default namespaces and unused declarations, missing/duplicate/cross-dialect entries, external/fragment targets, unsupported MIME types/parameters, wrong roots, unprocessed extensions, input/config capture, cancellation, budget failures, result independence and nested relationship isolation. Generated fixture checks and installed Node/TypeScript/browser ESM/global consumers use the public entry. A separate browser test covers Strict template classification, mismatched roots and zero external requests. Seven retained hash-matched LibreOfficeDev outputs now record main part, dialect, kind and macro-enabled type in the native inspection evidence; this is not a fresh native application round-trip.

Adversarial review examined five failure modes:

1. **Path and prefix heuristics:** the fixture main part deliberately has an unconventional path and extension; default/aliased prefixes and unused other-dialect declarations cannot determine the result.
2. **Ambiguous entry points:** two root relationships are rejected even if they share a target; nested officeDocument relationships do not count. External and fragment entry targets are rejected before classification.
3. **False conformance:** an identified root can contain unknown or schema-invalid descendants. The API and its tests explicitly demonstrate this boundary; full MCE handling, schema/semantic import and Strict-native validation remain open.
4. **Macro inference:** macroEnabled describes the main content type only. It is never named or documented as a macro-presence, safety or executable-content check. Executable-object policies remain ADV-11 work.
5. **Resource and state isolation:** all classification follows the bounded OPC pass, with no reparsing or new public payload/tree access. Input/config mutation, cancellation, malformed input and result mutation tests retain the existing ownership contract.

Final-state `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain passed (terminal exit 0). This includes 71 Parser cases, 6 browser-bundle cases, installed Node/TypeScript/browser consumers, visual tests and all fixture/parity suites. The separate `pnpm inspect:native-xml` run passed seven retained artifacts / 128 XML streams. Final documentation checks and `git diff --check` passed. IMP-01/IMP-02 and IMP-02.3 remain unchecked because session conformance, full dialect/MCE processing and semantic import are not delivered by entry identification.


## Explicit MCE projection and relationship integration — IMP-02.3 remains partial

The experimental `inspectMarkupCompatibility` API now applies explicit consumer namespace capabilities and application-defined extension islands to bounded XML. The [contract](../api/markup-compatibility.md) and [checked example](../examples/markup-compatibility.ts) define source/effective inventories, source element indices, decisions, failures and remaining restrictions. Private iterative projection implements Ignorable, ProcessContent, MustUnderstand, AlternateContent/Choice/Fallback and opaque islands without changing source trees. The shared XML NCName primitive moved from OPC into the XML substrate; no dependency was added.

OPC relationship XML declaring/using MCE is now projected before relationship validation. Its fixed consumer configuration understands OPC Relationships, with no application islands. Effective edges are validated and counted; raw XML budgets still cover every branch. `OpcPartInfo.markupCompatibility` makes selection/ignore decisions visible. Ordinary PresentationML bodies and content-type control XML do not silently acquire the same configuration. Source XML, opaque content and namespace binding provenance are not a preservation serializer; full presentation extension policy and semantic import remain open.

Seventeen new focused cases bring Parser to 88 tests. Tests inspect projected text/order as well as public metadata, a fixed eight-case nested-choice truth table, exact source indices, alias/rebinding/scope exit, wildcard unwrapping, active/inactive MustUnderstand, no-match forests, malformed lists/branch syntax, future MCE children, opaque subtree copy isolation, source budgets, configuration capture, cancellation and 1,200-level iterative traversal. OPC cases verify that unselected absent targets are not effective edges while all raw XML remains budgeted. The first test run caught an incorrect fixture element count (seven, not eight); the assertion was corrected after enumerating the source elements.

Browser coverage adds fallback selection, error parity and zero resource retrieval. Installed Node/TypeScript/browser ESM/global consumers cover public API/type availability. Re-inspection of the seven hash-matched LibreOfficeDev artifacts (128 XML streams) passed; this is a regression of existing producer artifacts, not new native evidence for every MCE feature.

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **Inactive-content failures versus syntax:** MustUnderstand and unknown ordinary namespaces are checked only in the effective projection; structural/list syntax remains checked outside opaque islands even in discarded branches. Unknown MCE policy rejects explicitly under the documented profile.
2. **Scope leakage:** prefix rebinding must not change an ancestor's resolved URI sets. Reversible scoped maps and alias/rebinding/sibling tests cover additive inheritance and restoration without per-node dictionary copies.
3. **False preservation:** opaque islands suspend MCE even on their own attributes, are copied without shared mutable subtree state, and retain unknown descendants. Ignored branches remain decisions, not a claim of preservation or consent to lossy save. Legacy PreserveElements/PreserveAttributes remain unsupported rather than silently ignored.
4. **Resource bypass and stack exhaustion:** discarded/opaque content still passes complete XML budgets. Source and effective traversals are iterative and cooperate with cancellation. Deep-tree and asynchronous cancellation cases exercise the actual projection, not just the XML parser.
5. **Invalid format output:** a generic MCE forest can have zero or multiple roots. OPC separately requires one effective Relationships root and validates its schema/profile and targets; relationship limits count effective edges while XML budgets count source markup. Unknown namespace declarations never automatically confer capability.

Final-state `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain passed with terminal exit 0. This includes all 88 Parser cases, 7 browser-bundle cases, installed Node/TypeScript/ESM/global checks, visual/measurement and existing fixture/parity suites. `pnpm inspect:native-xml` passed seven fixtures / 128 XML streams. Final documentation checks and `git diff --check` passed. IMP-02/IMP-02.3 stay unchecked pending complete presentation dialect/extension policy and conformance evidence.


## Effective PresentationML structure — IMP-03 remains partial

The public experimental `inspectPptx` now reads actual slide ordering, exact source EMU dimensions, numbering settings, hidden state and source identities, plus typed master/layout/notes/theme/handout references. The [structural contract](../api/pptx-inspection.md) defines validated fields, unresolved-inheritance findings and unimplemented semantics. It does not create a Core document or preservation session; metadata, notes content, sections and semantic drawing import remain open.

A private OPC source reader can retain XML trees within the original bounded pass. Existing envelope-only calls keep their inventory contract; the structural reader neither inflates/parses twice nor resets aggregate budgets. Part and source-relationship indexes, cached effective MCE roots and master-layout membership sets avoid repeated graph scans and recursive expansion of ordinary inverse relationships. Only Parser internals receive trees; no public AST, binary map or cross-package private import was added.

The implementation was checked against the official fifth-edition Part 1 Strict and Part 4 Transitional schemas. Both use slide size bounds 914400–51206400 EMUs and permit absent slide size; notes size is required and its coordinate type includes zero. The reader therefore retains absent slide dimensions and exact integer units instead of guessing display size. Source ID ranges and list cardinalities follow those schemas. Explicit references/type/root mismatches fail; missing implicit inheritance produces located findings, not fictitious defaults.

Sixteen focused structural cases bring Parser to 104 tests. Independent ZIP fixtures cover both dialects, arbitrary main filenames, reversed physical/relationship ordering, numeric identity/dimension bounds and lexical forms, duplicate identities/targets, bad r:id/type/fragment/external references, wrong part roots, show booleans, missing and contradictory inheritance, notes ownership/back-links, handout masters, MCE-controlled order and root alternatives, unknown extension islands, options/source snapshots and cancellation. A 250-slide case shares one cyclic layout/master graph while preserving independent page order and hidden flags. Generated corpus checks compare inspected dimensions and hidden state against authoring/Layout inputs, while installed Node/TypeScript/ESM/global and browser Strict fixtures exercise public exports.

The first native check caught a MIME comparison defect: the left side was lowercased but a camel-case expected subtype was not. Normalizing both sides fixed it without weakening type checks. The first cross-package dimension assertion also used a pixel conversion coefficient in a pt-based engine; it was corrected to the established 12700 EMU/pt contract. All seven generated fixture cases then passed. Hash-matched LibreOfficeDev evidence now records dimensions, ordered source slides and zero structural findings across all seven retained artifacts; this is re-inspection, not new native editing or round-trip evidence.

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **False page order:** only effective sldIdLst entries define sequence. Unused relationships are findings, filenames and ZIP order are irrelevant, and absent lists stay empty. Reversed and large shared-topology fixtures verify this.
2. **Identity/type confusion:** numeric uniqueness is checked after lexical parsing; relationship IDs remain source-local; targets must have the expected type/root/dialect. Shared notes, duplicate targets and inconsistent inverse relationships reject.
3. **Invented inheritance/defaults:** absent slide size stays absent; missing implicit layout/master/theme links are located findings. Full inherited visual state is not claimed. Source names are not inferred text titles.
4. **Resource/state regressions:** retention shares the original XML budgets and source snapshot. Relationship maps, membership sets, root caches and bounded cooperative processing avoid repeated parse work and recursive cycles; repeated calls return detached metadata.
5. **Scope overclaim:** entry identification, structural reading, semantic import and preservation sessions remain separate. MCE decisions are visible; drawing/notes/section/metadata contents are not silently called imported or preserved. The ADR and package catalog now distinguish live structural APIs from the pending import/save protocol.

The complete final `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` chain passed with terminal exit 0, including 104 Parser cases, 8 browser-bundle cases, installed consumers, visual/measurement and all fixture/parity suites. `pnpm inspect:native-xml` passed seven hash-matched fixtures / 128 XML streams. Final `pnpm docs:check` and `git diff --check` passed after documentation/catalog updates. IMP-03 and its child tasks remain unchecked until their complete import contracts are delivered.


## Document metadata inspection — IMP-03.4 remains partial

`inspectPptx` now returns package-linked core, extended and custom document metadata alongside its structural inventory. The [metadata profile](../api/pptx-inspection.md#document-metadata-profile) specifies all interpreted fields, source identities, value types and limitations. The implementation uses the already-retained bounded OPC XML source; no extra XML pass, Core dependency, public AST, resource fetch or source-preservation guarantee is added.

The standard review used the official ECMA-376 fifth-edition Strict/Transitional document-properties schemas and Part 1 §§15.2.12, 22.2–22.4, plus OPC 2021 §11. Core keywords retain mixed-language text/value segments, including inherited language and explicit resets. Date type QNames resolve namespace bindings. Extended scalar fields retain signed int32/boolean lexical values. Custom property IDs follow the schema int32 range rather than imposing an Office-specific minimum; format-ID/ID pairs and present names are checked for duplicates. Integer variant widths include exact signed/unsigned 64-bit bounds without Number coercion. Dates are deliberately returned without calendar validation; complex variants explicitly remain uninterpreted, not silently flattened or represented as preserved source.

Eighteen new Parser cases bring the total to 122. They exercise both dialects and unusual filenames, all core and extended property names, scalar lexical/range failures, 64-bit limits, optional names and prototype-like strings, GUID/identity failures, incorrect/missing/duplicate/external/fragment metadata links, mixed keyword languages, date QName aliases/rebinding, MCE profiles, source budgets including ignored branches, result detachment and traversal-specific cancellation for large custom/keyword lists. The initial budget assertion used an incorrect expected error code; it was corrected to the existing public `package-limit-exceeded` contract without changing production error behavior.

Generated-corpus tests compare inspected title/creator/keywords/revision and application/slide counts against authoring/Layout input. Browser fixtures compare both dialects against Node, retain a maximum ui8 value and assert zero network requests. Installed Node, TypeScript, browser ESM and global consumers exercise metadata availability and readonly/discriminated types. Seven hash-matched retained LibreOfficeDev artifacts (128 XML streams) passed re-inspection, with core/extended scalar text independently compared using Python ElementTree. The evidence file now records document metadata. This is inspection of existing saved artifacts, not a new native edit/save certification.

Adversarial review covered five concrete risks:

1. **Precision and language loss:** no date/decimal/64-bit value coercion; keyword segment boundaries and language resets survive. Fixtures assert exact source lexical strings and JSON serialization.
2. **Wrong property ownership or identity:** only correctly typed root relationships select metadata. Duplicate/orphan typed parts, mismatched dialects, unsafe relationship shapes and duplicate custom identities reject; absent groups remain absent.
3. **Misleading interpretation:** complex values have a discriminated uninterpreted result; dates are explicitly unvalidated, metadata counts are not actual slide counts, and signature/security fields do not imply verification or enforcement.
4. **Resource/cancellation regressions:** metadata shares source XML budgets even for discarded branches; custom-property and keyword traversal use cooperative checkpoints. Tests interrupt the metadata stage itself and verify subsequent reads remain usable.
5. **Self-confirming validation:** generated cases compare authoring input, malformed fixtures use independent ZIP construction, both browser dialects exercise public bundles, and native metadata has an independent XML reader comparison. No single screenshot or successful parse is treated as a round-trip guarantee.

IMP-03 and IMP-03.4 remain unchecked: complex-property interpretation, semantic Core/session mapping, notes/sections and preservation-save work are still open. The full roadmap goal remains active.

Final verification: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain finished with terminal exit 0. This includes 122 Parser cases, 9 browser-bundle cases, 26 visual/measurement cases, installed consumers and the generated-fixture/parity suites. Native metadata verification passed seven fixtures / 128 XML streams. Final documentation links/type examples and whitespace checks passed. Implementation: [metadata reader](../../packages/pptx-parser/src/metadata/read-metadata.ts); [focused cases](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/metadata.test.mjs); [native evidence](../testing/xml-inspection-evidence.json).


## Native section inspection — IMP-03.5 remains partial

`inspectPptx` now reads the named MS-PPTX native section collection, retaining source GUIDs, names, empty sections and ordered numeric slide references resolved against the effective presentation list. [The section profile](../api/pptx-inspection.md#native-sections-and-presentation-extensions) distinguishes absent collections, empty sections, schema-optional identities/names, duplicate names and partial/out-of-order membership findings. Present GUIDs and numeric identities are validated; duplicate or unresolved membership rejects. This does not equate native sections with Core's existing per-slide string labels, change exporter semantics, or implement edit/save reconciliation.

The official MS-PPTX section placement, section/list/slide-entry schemas and full 2010 namespace schema were reviewed. The section list requires at least one section; each section's required slide list may be empty. Name and GUID attributes are optional in the schema and are not synthesized. Nested p14:extLst uses the Transitional p:CT_ExtensionList child type even inside a Strict container. Known extension URI token whitespace is normalized; arbitrary URI casing is not rewritten.

The format reader uses a private MCE extension predicate only for the main presentation → extLst ownership path. Its section namespace capability does not propagate to other parts. Application ancestry skips transparent MCE/ProcessContent wrappers while raw-parent checks still enforce MCE syntax. Opaque ancestors and unrecognized nested extensions retain precedence. Public generic MCE options remain unchanged. Presentation/section extension descriptors expose only URI/status/direct root names, not raw payloads or preservation state.

Fifteen focused cases cover Strict/Transitional containers, source ordering and hidden-slide membership, empty/absent/invalid lists, optional/duplicate/empty names, GUID identity and token whitespace, numeric aliases/bounds/dangling/duplicate references, order/coverage findings, malformed envelopes and child grammar, scoped MCE selection and ignored attributes, root/extension AlternateContent and ProcessContent wrappers, reused URIs under unrelated owners, opaque unknown nested extensions, resource budgets, cancellation in the section traversal itself and result detachment. Parser now has 137 cases. The browser Strict fixture also asserts native GUIDs and member order; installed TypeScript consumers check readonly section/extension exports. Generated corpus checks ensure existing Core labels are not incorrectly promoted to native sections.

The optional [native section runner](../../scripts/inspect-native-sections.mjs) creates a full repository-authored two-slide text deck, injects three sections independently with Python ZIP handling, and uses a fresh native application profile/output directory. LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 saved the Introduction/Empty/Conclusion GUIDs, names and exact member order unchanged, then reopened the saved PPTX and exported PDF. Input and saved sections match independent ElementTree extraction; hashes, command output, platform and artifact location are in [the evidence record](../testing/native-sections-evidence.json). This covers one Transitional headless save/reopen fixture, not interactive editing, repair-dialog observation, visual fidelity, Strict-native or PowerPoint verification. The seven existing hash-matched native artifacts also passed re-inspection (128 XML streams).

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **Wrong owner grants interpretation:** a matching URI on a custom-show extension must not be treated as a presentation section extension. The first review identified an overly broad parent-only hook; it now checks the application grandparent too, with an unrelated-owner regression. MCE-transparent wrappers still retain the correct ownership path.
2. **Invented identities or membership:** schema-optional IDs/names remain absent; duplicate names never merge, empty sections remain distinct, and only effective presentation IDs resolve membership. Order/coverage inconsistencies are located findings rather than automatic repair.
3. **Unknown-content damage:** unknown top-level/nested islands remain opaque, including nested islands reusing the section URI. Descriptors do not claim source retention, editing, or compatibility support.
4. **Resource/aliasing failure:** map/set lookups avoid repeated slide scans; long loops checkpoint cancellation, source XML budgets remain enforced, findings are appended without unbounded argument spreading, and returned references are detached records.
5. **Overstated interoperability:** synthetic dialect coverage is separate from native evidence; a headless application save is not a PPTKit round-trip implementation. Core native-authoring mapping, section edits, unknown-content preservation and PowerPoint evidence remain open.

IMP-03/IMP-03.5 remain unchecked until their full Core/session and structural-save acceptance criteria are met. The overall roadmap goal remains active.

Final-state `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain passed with terminal exit 0 after the ownership-scope correction. This includes all 137 Parser cases, 9 browser-bundle cases, 26 visual/measurement cases, installed Node/TypeScript/browser consumers and generated-fixture/parity suites. Both hash-verified native section artifacts also match the final reader with no structural findings. Final documentation/type-example and whitespace checks passed.


## Structural import and original-byte save sessions — IMP-01.4 / IMP-03.6 remain partial

The experimental public `importPptx` API now requires explicit `projection: "structure"`. It maps source dimensions, selected string metadata, ordered slide identities and hidden flags into a valid Core document. The [session contract](../api/import-session.md) documents missing drawing/notes/theme semantics and Core defaults. Each source part has operation-specific findings; the report contains detached inspection data and source identities, but does not claim complete per-object capability reporting. Edit and rebuild requests return blocked reports with no output assurance. Original-byte no-op preparation requires normalized equality with the imported baseline.

Parser now imports Core through its public entry and declares its workspace dependency/reference. Browser bundles include Core internally for standalone usage; no reverse dependency, Exporter dependency or private cross-package import was introduced. Source bytes are copied before asynchronous work, including offset Buffer views. Shared buffers, macro-enabled/template/slideshow inputs, recognized VBA and signature payloads reject under the explicit input policy. Unknown content is retained without execution or implicit fetching; byte preservation is not content validity or signature verification.

Private sessions own source bytes and normalized baseline; plans own exact candidate bytes and snapshots. Ownership checks reject forged/cross-session plans. Concurrent preparation reserves output/count budgets before allocation, yields between 1 MiB chunks and releases reservations on cancellation, failure or disposal. Returned bytes are independent caller-owned copies. Plans remain reusable until disposed or stale; exact semantic undo restores freshness. No implicit rebasing occurs. Frozen reports remain usable after disposal, while sessions/plans reject serialization and cannot be transferred as preservation state.

Thirty session tests bring Parser to 167 cases. They cover both dialects, metadata/identity projection, empty decks, opaque resources and cyclic package relationships, byte identity, multiple plans, invalid consent, stale/forged/proxied/disposed plans, invalid Core state with located diagnostics, cancellation, concurrent reservations, multichunk output, source/option capture and recovery. Normalized snapshot tests reject cyclic/non-record data and enforce budgets; a 60-level shared DAG runs in a timeout-bounded child process to catch exponential comparison regressions. Equality distinguishes absent versus undefined fields and signed zero while ignoring record key insertion order. Wide-record comparison reserves child work before reading their values. A getter regression proves session limits are captured once, not repeatedly evaluated.

Public TypeScript consumers check required projection, readonly contracts, plan branding and consent types. Browser global tests exercise standalone import/save with no fetching; installed Node/ESM/global consumers and all seven generated fixtures verify original-byte output. The native re-inspection runner independently compares saved bytes against seven hash-matched retained LibreOfficeDev artifacts and records `noOpSession` evidence across 128 XML streams. This reuses existing native artifacts; it is not new interactive editing, playback or PowerPoint certification.

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **Incomplete projection mistaken for source content:** required mode and explicit warnings distinguish Core skeleton from semantic import. Edit/rebuild remain blocked, never silently export empty source pages.
2. **Aliasing or stale output:** invocation-time byte copies, private plan ownership, full normalized comparisons and final lifecycle checks cover mutation/disposal, including caller option getters. Saving returns fresh bytes.
3. **Unbounded traversal or concurrent allocation:** cycle rejection, shared-pair memoization, child-work reservations and aggregate output/count reservations have negative/recovery tests. Core normalization and key enumeration still allocate before all budget checks; documentation explicitly rejects a hard process-memory guarantee.
