4. **False loss consent or native fidelity:** only empty consent is accepted for current loss-free plans. Original-byte assurance does not imply editable unknown content, semantic validity, signature validity or native visual parity.
5. **Boundary and lifecycle leakage:** Core remains format-independent, reports do not expose source XML/buffers, disposal releases private retention, and installed/browser consumers verify the public dependency boundary. Package and capability documentation now consistently reflects the implemented Core dependency and limited session scope.

IMP-01/03/07/08/09 remain incomplete: full semantic mapping, preservation closure under edits, nonempty loss consent, semantic rebuild, independent package comparison and broader application evidence are still required. The full roadmap goal remains active.

Final verification: the complete `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` chain finished with terminal exit 0, including 167 Parser tests, browser bundles, installed consumers, visual/measurement and fixture/parity suites. `pnpm inspect:native-xml` passed seven retained fixtures / 128 XML streams. Documentation links/type examples and `git diff --check` also passed.


## Byte asset sources and explicit export resolution — AUT-04 remains partial

Core now accepts raw Uint8Array/Buffer views and explicitly encoded canonical Base64. Registration captures selected bytes into frozen Base64 records; normalized state remains detached and JSON serializable. Empty/noncanonical/shared-buffer input rejects before insertion. The additive IR v2 source variant and TypeScript union migration are documented in [byte asset contracts](../api/byte-assets.md). Existing ID/dedupeKey policy applies after source canonicalization; automatic cross-source content deduplication and asset lifecycle editing remain open.

Exporter default and Node entries now decode byte sources, classify PNG/JPEG/GIF headers and check declared MIME before packaging exact bytes under the matching extension. Renderer embeds the same supported content as self-contained data URLs, with explicit unsupported/mismatch placeholders. Header classification does not prove complete image validity or native GIF playback. Broader formats remain AUT-04.3. Existing path/URL inference behavior is retained; explicit resolvers provide the signature-checked route for those sources.

A public export resolver receives detached frozen asset metadata and an optional cancellation signal, returning copied byte content or explicit omission. Resolver failure never falls through to implicit filesystem/network loading. Invalid resources produce located recoverable warnings; cancellation is a hard rejection with the signal reason. Per-asset limits check stored decoded size before allocation, resolver buffers before copying, cumulative URL stream bytes, and both initial regular-file size and cumulative Node reads. Stream cancellation and file cleanup run on failure; total-process memory and arbitrary callback cancellation are not promised.

Core tests verify offset-view detachment, mutation resistance, JSON/normalization isolation, raw/Base64 identity equivalence, encoding padding/chunk boundaries through 100000 bytes, and failed-registration recovery. Export tests compare default/Node and URL/byte outputs, exact media content and shared references; cover invalid MIME/signatures, resolver ownership/omission/failure, size limits through all four loading routes, pre-abort, never-settling resolvers, stalled streams, reused stream buffers, resolver buffer mutation and independent retry. Preview tests cover self-contained URLs, placeholders and explicit overrides. Chromium actually decodes the PNG to 8×4, asserts zero fetches, checks abort, and compares browser/default/resolver output byte-for-byte against Node. Installed consumers exercise raw bytes and resolver output; negative TypeScript tests prevent mutable stored source and resolver-request assignments.

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **Mutable byte aliases in immutable state:** registration converts invocation-time byte snapshots to canonical strings; resolver results and received stream chunks are copied. Tests mutate original and reused buffers before later work.
2. **Wrong format labeled as an image:** byte/resolver header and declared MIME must agree; unsupported media is omitted with located diagnostics. Header-only JPEG/GIF tests are explicitly classification evidence, not decoder/native fidelity evidence.
3. **Implicit I/O after caller rejection:** undefined/throwing resolvers never trigger default loading. Browser tests forbid fetch; Node-only filesystem code remains outside browser bundles and Core.
4. **Resource growth and cancelled success:** bounded cumulative reads, before-copy checks, abortable waits and cleanup tests cover over-limit/stalled/uncooperative sources. Abortion cannot be converted into a successful partial package. Synchronous work and third-party callback resource ownership remain explicit limitations.
5. **Contract/documentation drift:** stored/input types are separate, Core/IR/Exporter/Renderer docs agree on the new variant, exhaustive source consumers have migration guidance, and public installed/typechecked examples cover the API. AUT-04 is not closed by this limited image profile.

The overall roadmap goal remains active. Byte-source foundation is now available to subsequent image extraction, cross-document asset and media work; broader content validation, formats, deduplication and lifecycle operations still require implementation and evidence.

Final verification: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain passed with terminal exit 0. Core has 33 cases, Exporter 26, Renderer 18, Parser 167, and browser bundles 11; visual/measurement, installed consumers and all fixture/parity suites also passed. Final documentation links/type examples and whitespace checks passed. No new native application image-format certification is claimed.


## Core asset lifecycle and byte content matching — AUT-04.2 remains partial

Core now exposes `replaceAsset`, `getAssetReferences`, `removeAsset` and `removeUnusedAssets`, plus opt-in `registerAsset(input, { deduplicate: "content" })`. The [lifecycle contract](../api/asset-lifecycle.md) specifies stable-ID full replacement, detached old snapshots, reference ownership, strict retention options and byte/metadata matching. No format-owned source graph, implicit resource loading, new dependency or IR version was introduced. Custom implementations of the public document interface must implement the added methods.

Reference inventory walks known image elements iteratively in slide/depth-first order, then layout order. Hidden content and unused reusable layouts remain references; inheritance is reported once at its authoring definition. Guarded removal rejects live uses and explicit retention. Bulk cleanup validates all retention IDs/options before mutation and preserves registry order using one compaction pass instead of repeated individual deletion. Unknown option keys reject so a misspelled keep-list cannot silently authorize cleanup. Retention is per operation; custom-data strings and unknown package relationships are not guessed.

Replacement captures input before commit, preserves identity/registry position and affects every shared use. Old asset views, normalized results and generated output remain detached. Full replacement intentionally clears omitted metadata; authored image frame/crop/transform values remain intact while contain/cover can resolve differently from new dimensions. Reentrant target replacement/removal causes the outer operation to reject, while unrelated registry index movement cannot overwrite a different asset. Top-level source getters are read once. General metadata semantics still validate through the established normalization path.

Content matching compares canonical byte strings and known metadata exactly, without hash-only equality. Explicit IDs and existing dedupe identities retain priority; URL/path resources are not fetched. Source/metadata property insertion order no longer creates false registration conflicts. Matching immediately reflects replacements and removals, with no stale content index.

Sixteen new Core lifecycle tests cover hidden/nested/layout references, dangling IDs, shared replacement, full metadata semantics, invalid-source/clone failures, reentrant updates, unknown/retained/live removal, identity reuse, immutable cleanup results, retention validation, exact byte/metadata matching, explicit IDs, no implicit URL/path deduplication, source getter capture and 2000 alternating retained/removed assets. Export tests verify PNG-to-GIF replacement updates shared relationship targets and MIME/extension without stale parts; explicit cleanup removes unused media from later output. Preview tests assert changed contain geometry while old SVG/Layout stays unchanged. Parser tests show new Core assets invalidate no-op plans and exact cleanup restores baseline validity without deleting original source bytes.

Browser consumers actually decode the replacement GIF to 1×1, validate shared-reference protection and explicit retention/cleanup, and generate a warning-free package. Installed consumers and negative TypeScript checks exercise the new methods, readonly reference records, replacement identity restrictions and rejection of force-style cleanup. These tests do not establish native GIF animation or arbitrary edit-preservation fidelity.

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **Deleting a shared or hidden resource:** complete known-graph scans include nested groups, hidden slides and every layout definition. No automatic deletion accompanies element/slide removal, and referenced deletion has no force override.
2. **Mistaking Core reachability for package closure:** explicit external keep-lists have validated Core IDs; original Parser source remains private. Unknown OOXML closure and edit-save reconciliation remain IMP-07/09 work and are not inferred from these tests.
3. **Replacing the wrong entry or mutating previous results:** capture-before-commit, previous-object identity checks and index re-resolution protect reentrant changes. Snapshot and aliasing tests cover old state, caller buffers and future output.
4. **False deduplication:** byte content and declared metadata must match, explicit IDs remain independent, URL/path inputs remain unloaded, and exact strings avoid collision-based merging. Case/default normalization and decoded-image equivalence are not claimed.
5. **Partial cleanup or quadratic deletion:** retention validation completes before deletion, unsupported option keys reject, and a single order-preserving compaction removes selected entries. Large alternating cleanup and repeated-pass tests protect ordering and completeness.

AUT-04 and AUT-04.2 remain open for cross-source resolution/deduplication, preservation-aware closure integration and richer lifecycle/edit policies. The full roadmap goal remains active.

Final verification: the complete `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` chain passed with terminal exit 0. Core has 49 tests, Parser 168, Exporter 28, Renderer 19, and browser bundles 12; installed Node/TypeScript/browser consumers, visual/measurement and fixture/parity suites also passed. Final documentation links/type examples and whitespace checks passed. Native image-edit/preservation certification remains outside this evidence.


## Same-document copy reference reconciliation — AUT-09.1 remains partial

The previous implementation regenerated copied IDs but left connector endpoints pointing at the source IDs. A direct reproduction copied a two-object slide and produced `missing-connector-target` on the new slide. Core now prepares a detached copy forest, allocates every fresh document-wide element identity first, then rewrites only modeled connector and action references. Existing insertion/materialization retains ownership and immutable storage responsibilities. Public method signatures and destination-index semantics remain unchanged.

Slide copies remap self-slide actions in element actions, text runs, shape text, table cells, nested groups and notes. Other slide/URL actions stay targeted as authored. Element/group copies remap connector targets inside their own copied subtree and retain outside targets; incoming source connectors are not redirected. Shared asset IDs, layout definitions and placeholder bindings remain shared. Plain text, names, section labels, tags and custom JSON are not searched or rewritten. The [copy contract](../api/copy-references.md) documents the corrected self-link behavior and the limits of same-document copying.

Fourteen focused Core tests cover forward/backward and nested references, connector-only/shape-only copies, all current action locations, shared layout/asset identity, custom-data isolation, repeated copying of copies, occupied generated-ID ranges, invalid destinations before ID allocation, preserved invalid-source diagnostics, empty hidden slides and a 600-element forward-reference graph. Source authoring objects remain unchanged, and deleting the original no longer invalidates internal copied connector targets.

Three [cross-package parity cases](../../scripts/copy-parity.test.mjs) independently assert expected Layout coordinates, SVG point sequences, emitted PPTX custom-path coordinates and OPC action relationships. They remove the original page/group and reorder copies before exporting. Notes verification extracts the actual text action relationship ID rather than mistaking the notes part's ordinary slide backlink for hyperlink evidence. `pnpm test:copy` runs this suite and is included in the full test chain. Browser and installed package consumers validate copied identities, self-links, resolved geometry and warning-free generation through public APIs.

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **Forward-reference or nested identity leakage:** the whole copied forest receives new IDs before reference rewriting, with occupied-ID and wide-graph tests. Internal links cannot depend on element order or retain removed source identities.
2. **Overreaching rewrites:** only known connector/action fields change. External targets, incoming source connections, shared templates/assets and arbitrary JSON/text remain untouched. Layout coordinate scope is unchanged; invalid original references remain diagnosable.
3. **Incomplete action coverage:** shape/table text, rich runs, group actions and notes receive the same self-slide mapping. Other-page links and URLs remain authored targets. Shared layout-owned actions are not silently specialized per copied slide.
4. **State or allocation corruption:** source objects are cloned, stored copies remain immutable, unknown targets/invalid indices reject before insertion, and invalid destinations do not consume generated copy IDs. Repeated copies remain independent and unique.
5. **False output evidence:** coordinate expectations are independently calculated, SVG and XML are inspected, OPC verifies actual action edges, and browser/installed consumers exercise the API. Existing custom-path connector export is not claimed to become native attached-connector editing, nor does this establish unknown-object preservation.

AUT-09 and AUT-09.1 stay open for their full template/advanced-object dependencies, cross-document reuse and format-preservation integration. The overall roadmap goal remains active.

Final verification: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain finished with terminal exit 0. Core has 63 cases and browser bundles 13; all three copy parity cases, installed consumers, visual/measurement and existing package/fixture/parity suites passed. Final documentation links/type examples and whitespace checks passed. Native application edit/playback certification remains separate.


## Experimental native straight connectors — AUT-02.3 remains partial

The exporter now accepts `connectorMode: "native"` through both public entries, with default `"path"` output retained. The [profile contract](../api/native-connectors.md) defines supported straight connectors, cardinal attachment sites for text rectangles/rect/roundRect/ellipse, same-part and same-immediate-group scope, and explicit whole-connector path fallback. Layout retains detached format-independent endpoint identities; only Exporter assigns OOXML identities. Forward references are resolved through preallocated visible drawing IDs, isolated per part and invocation.

Fourteen focused [exporter tests](../../packages/pptx-exporter/test/native-connectors.test.mjs) cover the site tables, reverse directions and zero extents, transformed ancestor groups, unsupported local transforms/routes/targets, hidden ancestors, layout ownership, copied targets, concurrency, generated placeholders and omitted images. The latest regression removes/reinserts a target with changed position and size, verifies independently calculated endpoints, native bounds/flips and changed drawing IDs, and confirms previous output is unchanged. This is API regeneration evidence, not interactive movement in Office. Browser consumers verify Node/browser byte equality and installed consumers exercise public options/types.

The [native application record](../testing/native-connectors-evidence.json) contains hashes, versions and logs for LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 headless save and reopen to PDF. Independent ZIP/XML inspection found ten original and ten saved native connectors and preserved all five attached target-name/site-index pairs. The application changed coordinates, zero extents and grouped geometry representation. Exact visual geometry, interactive attachment editing and PowerPoint remain unverified. The optional runner and named fixture make this experiment repeatable without introducing native tools into portable CI.

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **Wrong connection-site indices:** cardinal tables are tested separately for each supported target; ellipse indices account for diagonal sites. Center anchors explicitly fall back.
2. **Dangling or colliding IDs:** forward targets, copied targets, hidden subtrees, missing images, generated placeholders, concurrent output and reordered replacement targets are covered. Supported attachments use actual part-local drawing identities.
3. **Incorrect coordinate scope:** cross-group/cross-part references and local target/connector transforms fall back with located reasons. A transformed common ancestor is tested separately; fallback does not claim to repair unsupported Layout coordinates.
4. **Default compatibility or boundary leakage:** explicit path/default output bytes match, prior output and Core input remain detached, and Layout provenance contains authoring IDs rather than XML. Public docs describe the additive Layout field.
5. **Overstated application support:** attachment inventory after headless save is reported separately from geometry, native interactive editing and playback. No application parity claim or task closure follows from conversion success.

AUT-02 and AUT-02.3 remain open for bent/curved routes, transformed target sites, import/edit-save integration and native interactive evidence. The full roadmap objective remains unfinished.

Final verification on 2026-10-24: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain finished with terminal exit 0. Core has 63 tests, Layout 123, Parser 168, Exporter 42 and Renderer 19; browser bundles have 14 cases and visual/measurement has 26. Installed consumers and all existing fixture/parity/copy suites passed. `git diff --check` passed. Application evidence remains limited to the recorded headless inventory experiment.


## Transformed connector targets — AUT-02.3 remains partial

Layout now evaluates target-local cardinal anchors by applying flips before clockwise rotation about the target frame center. Previously it used only the untransformed frame, so preview/path export geometry could disagree with a native target site. Final Layout and intrinsic group fitting share this corrected endpoint evaluation. Free endpoints/routes retain their authored coordinates; group children remain in local coordinates and inherit the ancestor transform once. Same-scope native straight export now accepts rotated/flipped supported targets without changing the local connection-site identity.

Three focused Layout cases independently verify all cardinal/center anchors, combined rotation/flip, common transformed groups and inherited layout targets. A native export regression covers seven transform combinations including 45-degree and negative rotation, independently calculated bounds, unchanged input and target site IDs. Native application evidence for transformed targets remains outstanding; prior headless results cover the earlier profile only.

Adversarial review checked five failure modes: flip/rotation order (combined-transform expectations), confusing local versus world coordinates (transformed-group test), inherited target lookup (layout target test), mismatched XML site versus coordinates (independent exported bounds and retained site identity), and overclaiming compatibility (default geometry correction documented; cross-group/cross-part native links and connector-local transforms still fall back). No new public API or dependency was introduced. AUT-02.3 and the full roadmap remain unfinished.

Verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build` and the focused native suite passed, followed by terminal exit 0 for `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain. Layout now has 126 cases and Exporter 43; existing visual/measurement, installed consumers, browser and fixture/parity suites passed. Final docs check and whitespace check passed.


## Partial slide-local content import — IMP-04 remains partial

`importPptx` now accepts explicit `projection: "content"` in addition to the unchanged structural profile. The [contract](../api/content-import.md) maps direct rich text, seven preset shapes and nested groups, including transforms, direct paints/strokes, paragraph/run styles, bullets, frame settings and nonzero group child origins. Painted text boxes remain shapes with text. The structural reader exposes a private context-based orchestration function so inspection and content conversion share one bounded OPC/XML/MCE source pass; no new runtime dependency or XML-bearing Core state was added.

Parser reports retain source/Core identities and effective XML paths. A consumed-node/attribute audit reports unknown properties on known objects and uninterpreted subtrees, with owning identities where available. Each mapped object and unknown property/family receives distinct operation findings; only mapped fields are declared editable. Inheritance/default projection values are separately marked fallback. Unknown objects remain in original source bytes, not fabricated Core elements. Edit/rebuild saves remain blocked, normalized changes invalidate no-op plans and exact restoration permits them again.

Seventeen new Parser cases cover both namespace dialects, literal Unicode/XML/CDATA/whitespace, empty paragraphs, direct styles and numbering, seven shape presets, painted text, nested/nonzero group coordinates, unknown effects/attributes/namespaces, opaque extension precedence, duplicate IDs even within omitted groups, malformed source fields, MCE fallback/aliases, unsupported valid margins/indent/zero child extents, cancellation, report/XML budgets, getter capture and session isolation/freshness. A browser compares full reports/normalized content with Node and verifies exact source bytes and imported rendered text. Installed runtime/type consumers exercise the public option. Two new `pnpm test:import` cases independently compare supported fields and actual exported geometry/styles, then test fresh Core-edit output separately from refused preservation edits.

The optional [native producer evidence](../testing/content-import-evidence.json) uses a LibreOffice-produced save of the repository fixture. Three objects map and both nonempty text bodies match independent Python source/fresh-export inventories. No-op bytes are identical and the fresh projection reopens headlessly to PDF. The source still produces 32 unmapped-property and three inheritance/default findings. The runner records hashes/version/logs and retains the full report in a fresh artifact directory. No visual, interactive or PowerPoint certification is inferred.

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **False round-trip claims:** structural/content modes stay explicit; mapped-field editability is separate from full object/part fidelity, and edit/rebuild refuse without source reconciliation. Fresh export and source no-op have independent tests and documentation.
2. **Silent property loss:** unknown nodes/attributes, effects, differing script fonts, theme fonts, run alpha and valid unrepresentable margins/indent remain located findings; byte-preserved no-op retains the source. Opaque extension contents are not mistaken for source drawing identities.
3. **Coordinate or identity corruption:** nested child origins rebase once, transforms remain local, IDs remain part-local at source and document-wide in Core, duplicates reject before publication, including omitted groups. Direct cross-package fields and native XML dimensions are compared independently.
4. **Unbounded/unsafe interpretation:** existing XML/MCE/package budgets cover all branches, report budgets reject rather than truncate, iterative source audit/identity scans and cooperative projection checkpoints support cancellation. No source relationship, hyperlink or media is implicitly fetched/executed.
5. **Lifecycle/compatibility drift:** existing structural sessions retain their behavior, getter/options/source snapshots and foreign/stale plan guards remain intact, reports are frozen and detached, and public Node/browser/type consumers plus the original session suite run against the added mode.

IMP-04.1/04.2/04.4 and parent IMP-04 remain open for complete inheritance, image extraction, connector/action mapping, fuller object capability coverage and broader producer evidence. Preservation closure, edit-save, remaining advanced families and the full roadmap goal remain unfinished.

Final verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain finished with terminal exit 0. Parser has 185 cases (17 new content cases), browser bundles have 15, and both import parity cases passed alongside existing package, visual/measurement, installed-consumer and fixture/parity suites. After extracting the shared named fixture and adding the native runner, typecheck/lint, `pnpm test:import` and `pnpm test:packages` again passed with terminal exit 0. The optional native experiment also finished with terminal exit 0 and recorded its limited positive results and remaining findings. Final documentation links/typed examples and whitespace checks passed.


## Embedded raster content import — IMP-04.2/04.4 remain partial

The content projection now maps rectangular embedded PNG/JPEG/GIF pictures through public Core image/asset contracts. Supported fields include direct frame/rotation/flips, nonnegative source crop with a positive remaining area, explicit stretch and one fixed alpha multiplier within 0–100%. Nested pictures reuse group child-origin rebasing. Unknown effects, locks, attributes and source semantics remain located findings; unsupported placement, clipping and image headers omit only the affected picture. The [contract and example](../api/content-import.md) distinguish these fields from full picture fidelity.

The original bounded OPC pass can privately retain its verified payload map for content projection. Parser resolves validated image relationships, reads PNG IHDR/GIF logical-screen/JPEG frame dimensions and registers immutable Base64 bytes. JPEG segment lengths prevent false frame matches inside APP/comment/table payloads; repeated references reuse header results. Canonical source parts own asset IDs across slides/groups, while identical bytes in distinct source parts keep distinct identities. Asset provenance records the source binary part and each drawing/relationship reference. No new package/dependency or format-bearing Core state was introduced.

New optional session limits bound candidate image bytes before scanning/registration (16 MiB per asset, 64 MiB aggregate by default). Existing ZIP/XML limits remain in force. External-only pictures create no URL assets and trigger no loads in import, preview or fresh export; embedded-plus-linked pictures use only their internal representation and report unmapped updates. Images remain ordinary editable Core assets after session disposal, while source-preservation plans retain existing freshness/ownership rules.

Thirteen focused [image import cases](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/content-images.test.mjs) cover both dialects, namespace aliases/MCE selection, exact PNG/GIF bytes, JPEG header segment skipping, dimensions and truncation, crop/alpha/transform semantics, shared and distinct source parts, external links, wrong-type/mode/missing/fragmented references, unknown effects, unsupported placement, budget boundaries, cancellation, isolation, replacement, stale plans and exact restoration. The complete original 17 content tests still pass.

Two additional [integration cases](../../scripts/import-content-parity.test.mjs) compare public Core semantics and independently inspect emitted ZIP/XML geometry, crop, opacity, actual media bytes and relationship targets through three fresh projection exports; uncompressed package size remains stable. Shared PNG-to-GIF replacement affects every imported use and fresh output without altering original bytes or enabling edit-preservation saves. Installed Node/TypeScript consumers exercise image mapping and limit options. A new Chromium case checks full Node/browser report/output parity, actual 8×4 PNG decoding, exact authored/imported raster pixels with crop/opacity/group transforms, and zero external network requests.

The optional [native experiment](../../scripts/inspect-image-import.mjs) completed against LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 on macOS. Independent Python ZIP/XML inspection matches all three imported picture resource digests, source crop fractions and local frames at integer-EMU precision; the same inventory survives fresh projection export. No-op bytes match exactly, and fresh output reopens to PDF. The [evidence record](../testing/image-import-evidence.json) includes hashes, application/OS versions, commands/logs and remaining findings. LibreOffice's changes to source geometry/crop are recorded rather than mistaken for authoring parity. Native visual matching, interactive picture editing, PowerPoint and general image decoding are unverified.

Adversarial review addressed five failure modes:

1. **Crop preview falsely passing without source dimensions:** the initial cross-package check exposed `image-crop-degraded`. Bounded intrinsic header dimensions now supply Layout/Renderer; exact Chromium pixels independently exercise crop, opacity and nested transforms.
2. **Implicit external loading:** external-only references never become Core URL assets; a real browser observes no requests through import, preview and fresh export. Mixed internal/external pictures retain explicit update-loss findings.
3. **Incorrect source deduplication or unbounded copying:** sharing follows canonical part identity, not filenames or byte hashes. Same-target aliases count once across slides; distinct equal-byte parts are charged independently; byte-limit boundaries and malformed header cases are covered before registration.
4. **Silent picture loss or false decoding claims:** unsupported crops/masks/tile/rotation semantics are omitted with located reasons, unknown modifiers remain audited, and MIME/header/dimension reading is explicitly separated from pixel/EXIF/color/animation validation. Exact source no-op preserves all original parts.
5. **Save/session contamination:** imported asset replacement invalidates old no-op plans; exact restoration permits them again. Concurrent sessions, caller-buffer mutation, immutable stored bytes and unsupported edit/rebuild modes retain their existing guarantees.

IMP-04.2/04.4 and parent IMP-04 remain open for broader image semantics, full inheritance, remaining object families and required application evidence. Preservation closure, edit-save, SmartArt, media, motion and the full roadmap remain unfinished.

Verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain finished with terminal exit 0. Parser now has 198 cases, browser bundles 16, and import integration four; all 26 existing Chromium visual/measurement cases, installed tarball consumers and the remaining workspace/fixture/parity suites passed. The independent native image experiment also exited 0. Local logs are `.tmp/image-import-{full-build,typecheck,lint,full-tests,native}.log`.


## Connector and click-action import — IMP-04.3 remains partial

The content projection now maps straight/line connector endpoints after the full drawing forest is parsed. Forward references work; supported same-scope text rectangles, rectangles, rounded rectangles and ellipses retain source identities and cardinal anchors. Source rotation/flips resolve once into free endpoint geometry. Unsupported/missing targets, sites, scopes and routes keep cached free endpoints with located findings; these cases do not retain attachment semantics. Fresh export defaults to path connectors; callers must explicitly request `connectorMode: "native"` to write the supported native attachment profile.

Direct object and text-run click actions map safe absolute HTTP/HTTPS/mailto URLs and resolved slide relationships. The parser never fetches URLs or executes source actions. Unsupported macro/program/file/relative/media/hover/sound and unresolved actions remain source-only with findings. Exporter now emits explicit `ppaction://hlinksldjump` and same-directory `slideN.xml` targets from slide parts; notes actions retain sibling-relative paths. SVG preview exposes metadata without performing navigation.

The focused coverage adds 17 Parser cases, two Exporter action cases, two import parity cases and one browser parity case. The six import integration tests independently inspect relationships/targets/sites, exercise three fresh native projection rounds, test copy/delete/self-link behavior and confirm stale-plan handling. Installed Node/TypeScript consumers cover the public parser/exporter entries.

The optional [native reference experiment](../testing/reference-import-evidence.json) converts the fixture with LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 on macOS. Independent ZIP/XML inventory confirms three attachment references and four surviving direct actions map into Core and survive fresh native projection export. Exact no-op source bytes match, and the fresh projection reopens to PDF. The original fixture has six actions: LibreOffice drops the connector and group object actions, removes tooltips from surviving actions and normalizes URL escaping. These are recorded as producer changes. No interactive click, attachment movement, visual fidelity or PowerPoint claim follows from this headless evidence.

Adversarial review checked five likely failure points:

1. **Double transforms:** connector flips/rotation are flattened once; transformed target anchors use the established Layout calculation, and independent geometry/site checks cover grouped references.
2. **Forward or wrong-scope identity resolution:** all drawings are indexed before attachment resolution; source IDs are part-local, Core IDs are document-wide, and cross-group/unmapped references produce findings.
3. **Unsafe/dangling actions:** only direct safe URLs and resolved slide links map; relationships are type/mode checked, unresolved or unsafe actions remain source-only, and nothing is fetched or executed.
4. **Copy/delete/stale plans:** focused Core/Parser/integration/browser cases exercise remapping, deletion, moved targets, source immutability and stale save-plan rejection.
5. **False native/round-trip claims:** default export remains path mode; imported attachments require explicit native mode, free endpoint fallback is not attachment preservation, source no-op remains byte-identical, edit/rebuild remains blocked, and native producer losses are explicit.

IMP-04.3/04.4 and parent IMP-04 remain open for other routes/sites/actions, complete inheritance, broader object capability coverage and multiple producer/application evidence. Preservation closure, edit-save, SmartArt, media, animation and the complete roadmap remain unfinished.

Verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm test` and the optional `node scripts/inspect-reference-import.mjs` all exited 0. Parser has 215 cases, Exporter 45, browser bundles 17 and import integration six; the full suite also passed visual/measurement, installed consumers, fixtures, composition, copy, browser and parity checks. `rtk git diff --check` and the documentation links/contracts/examples check passed.


## Speaker-notes content import — IMP-03.3 remains partial

Core now exposes `slide.setNotes()` for atomic replacement/clear with a detached immutable snapshot. The experimental content projection maps one direct notes body placeholder's local paragraphs, runs, supported URL/slide actions and source identity into Core. Source inspection still reports notes-page geometry, page artwork, fields, footer/header/slide-number placeholders, notes-master/theme/list inheritance and unsupported actions. Ambiguous direct bodies and inherited-only text remain unmapped. Notes remain absent from slide SVG; edit-preservation and semantic rebuild are blocked.

The [notes contract](../api/notes-import.md) and [example](../examples/notes-import.ts) document the boundary, source identity kind and consumer migration. Export retains empty paragraphs with authored style/actions while omitting a plain default paragraph synthesized by normalization. Core, Parser, Exporter, browser and public installed consumers exercise replacement, clearing, immutability, malformed/ambiguous bodies, IDs, MCE aliases, actions, source paths, cancellation/budgets, copy/self-link remapping, undo, stale plans, exact no-op bytes and repeated fresh exports. Fresh output is independently inspected for notes XML/relationships and compared with normalized re-import; notes are verified absent from the slide SVG and external actions are never loaded.

The optional [LibreOfficeDev experiment](../testing/notes-import-evidence.json) records version, platform, hashes, independent Python ZIP/XML inventories, no-op equality and fresh PDF reopening. It found that LibreOffice splits an authored line break into paragraphs, converts notes slide jumps into relative external URLs, strips tooltips and normalizes URL escaping. The two converted relative links remain source-only with diagnostics; supported HTTPS text/action inventory survives fresh output. This validates a bounded direct-body projection, not notes-page rendering or interactive/native editing.

Adversarial review addressed five risks:

1. **Misidentifying body text:** only one direct `p:sp` with a `p:ph type="body"` is mapped. Names/order, arbitrary text boxes and nested group placeholders do not infer notes; multiple candidates and missing local text remain source-only.
2. **Confusing owner relationships with links:** explicit relationship IDs referenced by click actions are separated from the implicit slide backlink; self-links can share the owning slide relation, while conflicting or multiple owners reject.
3. **Silent loss of blank formatting:** exporter tests cover empty styled/action runs, while generated plain defaults are omitted. Pagination parity initially caught a byte drift between synthesized default paragraphs and explicit empty lists; the narrower content predicate restored equivalent package output.
4. **Unsafe action execution or false fidelity:** only existing supported safe URL/slide actions map. Relative links rewritten by LibreOffice remain source-only; no URL is fetched, and notes-page/master/effect provenance is not claimed.
5. **Mutable or stale Core state:** setter snapshot/clear failures are atomic, copy remaps self-links, undo restores authoring state, stale source plans reject after edits and exact source no-op bytes remain unchanged.

IMP-03.3 and parent IMP-03 remain open for notes-page/master semantics, shared/inherited placeholder resolution, native notes rendering and preservation-aware editing. Broader import/edit-save, SmartArt, transitions, animation, media and the full roadmap goal remain unfinished.

Verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, the full `pnpm test` chain and `git diff --check` passed. The initial full-suite run caught the pagination notes-body difference described above; after correction, the focused 10-case composition/pagination suite and the complete suite passed. The focused notes tests comprise four Core, fifteen Parser and one Exporter case, plus two new import integration cases and one browser parity case. The optional LibreOffice experiment exited successfully and recorded its producer-side losses.

The next IMP-03.4 metadata increment also maps the exact aggregate keyword string into one Core keyword entry and maps only nonnegative safe integer revisions. Mixed keyword segment/language structure receives a separate unsupported finding; oversized or malformed revision lexicals remain exact in the inspection report and are not rounded into Core numbers. Structural session tests cover both safe and unsafe revision values and mixed-language keyword reporting.


## Native section member-label projection — IMP-03.5 remains partial

Both import modes now map a native section member's decoded name to its Core slide label by the validated source numeric slide identity. The mapping does not infer names from GUIDs, filename/page position or adjacent entries, rewrite the authoritative slide order, or merge equal names. Explicit empty names survive as empty strings; absent names and unsectioned slides remain unlabeled. The [contract](../api/import-session.md#native-section-member-labels) explicitly separates this mapping from a native section collection.

Each source section receives operation-specific `section.native-collection` findings, including empty entries. Only exact source no-op is preserved; GUID identity, empty sections, collection ordering and nested extensions are not fabricated as Core identities/custom data. Named members get independent `section.label` findings with the Core slide ID, source GUID when present, member relationship and section-extension-qualified source path. Fresh export is classified fallback for labels: the exporter currently writes PPTKit slide metadata, not a native section list. Label edit/preview and preservation edits remain unsupported.

Seven focused Parser cases cover both dialects/projections, absent versus empty collections, missing versus empty names, repeated Unicode names, partial/reversed membership, source identity/path reports, copy/move/delete/undo and stale plans, report-budget boundaries and cooperative cancellation/recovery. A fresh-output integration case independently inspects slide metadata and confirms the native collection is absent after export/reimport. Browser report and no-op/stale/undo behavior match Node with zero network requests; the isolated installed Node consumer reads a native-section source fixture through public package exports.

The [native runner/evidence](../testing/native-sections-evidence.json) now verifies Core member labels against an independent Python XML membership inventory after LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 save. All three source entries, including the empty section, survived that native conversion; two member labels match by source IDs and exact no-op bytes match the native-produced package. The experiment retains its prior scope limits: no interactive collection editing, repair-dialog observation, Strict-native or PowerPoint claim.

Adversarial review checked five likely failures:

1. **Wrong membership by page index or duplicate name:** reversed/partial and repeated-name fixtures prove mapping by numeric source slide ID; inspected page/section ordering is unchanged.
2. **Empty or anonymous sections silently becoming a Core collection:** source absence, empty entries and missing/empty names have separate tests/reports. No Core section identity is invented; source GUIDs remain report provenance.
3. **Fresh export being called native preservation:** independent ZIP/XML and reimport tests prove only PPTKit label metadata is generated and native sections are absent; source reports classify that export as fallback/unsupported at the appropriate granularity.
4. **Structural mutations contaminating source bytes:** labels follow existing Core move/copy/delete behavior, source reports stay immutable, old plans reject after reordering and exact semantic undo restores original-byte output. Edit-preservation mode still blocks.
5. **Unbounded projection or stale consumer behavior:** constant report rows per section/member use the shared finding budget, all collection/member traversals checkpoint for cancellation, source paths identify the named extension, and Node/browser/installed public consumers exercise the new behavior.

IMP-03.5 and the parent roadmap remain open for a native Core section collection, independent empty-section identities, explicit section boundary/membership editing, native export, cross-document identity mapping and preservation-aware structural reconciliation. The current label projection is recorded as partial progress only.

Final review also tightened the prior metadata slice: a single keyword `<value>` wrapper now reports lost segment structure even without language annotations, and revision tests cover zero, the safe-integer boundary, overflow, sign/whitespace/leading-zero lexicals and empty input. These lexical forms stay in the source report when they cannot map canonically.

Verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain exited 0. Final Parser count is 240, browser bundles 19, import integration nine, and the 26 Chromium visual/measurement cases plus installed-package, fixture, composition and copy suites passed. The expanded native section experiment also exited 0. Portable full-suite output is retained locally at `.tmp/section-import-final-test.log`; documentation links/typed examples and whitespace checks passed.

## Direct table content import — IMP-06.1 remains partial

The [table profile](../api/table-import.md) now maps authored grid columns/row heights, rectangular merge anchors, rich-text cell content/clicks, direct fills, equal four-edge borders, margins/anchoring and graphic-frame transforms. Parser validates the complete physical grid before interpreting anchor text/actions and converts it to compact Core rows. Missing optional cell bodies become empty text. Nonpositive dimensions, sparse/excess physical cells and nonempty continuation content remain source-only; malformed mapped values, out-of-grid/overlapping/orphan merges and duplicate singleton nodes fail with typed package errors. Unknown table styles, per-edge/continuation properties and theme inheritance stay source-reported.

Source frame and grid dimensions remain independent; disagreement beyond one EMU marks preview fallback. Exporter now retains native table rotation/flips, using PresentationML frame transforms without changing identity transforms or ordinary drawing namespaces. No package/dependency/public authoring shape was added; XML/source ownership remains in Parser. The [checked example](../examples/table-import.ts) explicitly distinguishes fresh projection export from blocked preservation editing.

Twenty-one [focused Parser cases](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/tables.test.mjs) include Strict/Transitional and URI combinations, MCE aliases, optional/empty text, rich text/actions, style precedence, merge failures, nonpositive/sparse grids, duplicate IDs, nested coordinates, report budgets, cancellation, isolation, undo and stale plans. A deterministic geometric-bisection oracle generates forty disjoint rectangular partitions independently of the streaming occupancy algorithm and checks every imported anchor/span. The [Exporter regression](../../packages/pptx-exporter/test/table-transforms.test.mjs) verifies table rotation/flips and ordinary shape namespaces.

Two new cross-package cases compare full supported table semantics over three fresh export/import rounds, independently inspect physical cells/merge flags/grid/transform/style/action XML, verify stable resource size and copied cell self-links, edit cells and confirm preservation modes remain blocked. The browser case compares complete Node/browser documents, reports, fresh/no-op bytes and exact rendered pixels, exercises cell edits/undo and observes zero network requests. Installed tarball consumers import merged cells/transforms/self-links and re-export/re-import through public entries. An initial consumer run caught a duplicate local variable in the test extension; the existing session was reused, and the final installed/full suite passed.

The [native experiment/evidence](../testing/table-import-evidence.json) uses LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0, independent Python ZIP/XML inventories and exact integer-EMU/angle comparisons. Both produced tables map, with six and one anchors, survive fresh projection export and reopen to PDF; original-byte no-op passes. The producer changes frames/row metrics, removes flips and collapses the fully merged 2×2 grid into one cell before import. Those changes remain recorded in separate original/native inventories. Its frame/grid discrepancy receives preview fallback. No interactive editing, native table-style fidelity, repair-dialog observation, PowerPoint or arbitrary producer claim follows.

Adversarial review addressed five likely failures:

1. **Wrong merge/data ownership:** independent geometric partitions, active-merge expiry, wholly merged rows and malformed-continuation cases cover membership and ordering. Nonempty continuation text/fields/breaks never silently disappear into anchor-only cells.
2. **Invented geometry or invalid-input confusion:** dimensions are read as checked source EMUs. Schema-range nonpositive sizes are unrepresentable source-only values; malformed/out-of-range lexicals reject. Missing optional text does not fabricate visible content. Frame/grid mismatch remains explicit.
3. **False style fidelity:** incomplete/unequal edge strokes become neutral projection paint with source findings; theme/table-style rules, diagonal lines, text direction and unknown frame properties are not claimed mapped. Standard cell margins/anchor override compatible legacy body values.
4. **Reference, state or resource corruption:** table/cell actions are located and never executed; copy remaps self-links. Original reports/source remain detached, edits invalidate plans, undo restores exact bytes, and budget/cancellation/disposal paths pass without cross-session leakage.
5. **Circular self-verification or native overclaims:** independent XML/partition and Python inventories complement Parser/Exporter comparisons. Three-round resource checks, actual Chromium pixels, installed consumers and producer-loss records bound the evidence; fresh output remains distinct from preservation save.

Verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, the full `pnpm test` chain and the optional `node scripts/inspect-table-import.mjs` exited 0. Parser has 261 cases, Exporter 47, browser bundles 20, import integration 11; all 26 visual/measurement cases, installed-package, fixture/performance, chart, composition and copy suites pass. Full portable output is retained locally at `.tmp/table-import-full-test.log`, build output at `.tmp/table-import-build.log`.

IMP-06.1 and its parent remain open for complete native style/theme semantics, per-edge borders, remaining inherited contracts and interactive/native application gates. AUT-05 structural editing/reusable styles, chart import, preservation edit-save, SmartArt, media/motion and the overall roadmap goal remain unfinished. This record is evidence for the direct table slice, not completion of the full backlog.


## Bounded chart data projection — 2026-10-25

Related open tasks: IMP-06.2.1–06.2.5, AUT-06.2 and IMP-06. This entry records a bounded implementation; inherited dependencies and full data/format/application gates remain open.

The [chart profile](../api/chart-import.md) now maps single-family 2D bar/line/pie charts through retained effective chart trees in both OOXML dialects. Source series orders/point identities are validated and sorted, one category/value axis pair is checked, direct styles/title/legend/markers and expressible spacing map, and all unsupported properties retain chart-part paths/source-object identities. Native layout/format/inheritance is not reproduced; preview is marked fallback. Source-only charts have no fabricated Core element ID. Unknown properties receive per-operation capabilities; exact no-op preserves them, while semantic import/export/edit-save does not claim support.

[ADR 0002](../architecture/decisions/0002-chart-data-projection.md) makes complete caches authoritative, compares supported workbook stored ranges, reports disagreement, and permits workbook fallback only for absent caches. Existing sparse/empty caches, error/nonfinite data, negative pie values, missing names/categories and incompatible arrays stay source-only. Formula cells use stored results without recalculation; external targets are never fetched. XLSX ZIP/XML/OPC, relationships/content types/cells and shared strings are bounded/validated using existing Parser adapters. Workbook inspection shares per-part/aggregate expanded-byte budgets, while cache points, cells/shared strings and every referenced cell read share a cancellable data-work budget. Distinct source workbooks count separately; repeated references cannot amplify fallback arrays beyond the work budget.

Fresh output derives cache and embedded workbook from the same current Core arrays, using separate chart/workbook pairs per element. Native chart frame rotation/flips now serialize through the shared PresentationML transform helper. A discovered late-MCE freeze defect is fixed by detaching public structural report snapshots and including later content-part decisions in the final content report. Chart extension islands also remain opaque to nested MCE requirements. No Core/IR XML or package dependency was added. Preservation edit/rebuild remains blocked.

Evidence:

- [22 independent chart test groups](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/charts.test.mjs): Strict/Transitional, explicit order/indices, lexical numbers/CDATA, aliases/effective MCE/opaque extensions, titles/styles/markers/pie colors, malformed/sparse caches, worksheet content/relationship errors, quoted/horizontal references, cache/workbook disagreement/missing/error/MCE cases, external targets, shared/distinct workbook budgets, repeated-range amplification, source identities, nested group transforms, cancellation and no-op bytes.
- [Two integration groups](../../scripts/chart-import-parity.test.mjs): three fresh rounds with independently inspected complete caches/worksheet cells, frame transforms/relationships, series deletion/reorder and category changes, no stale source data, stable expanded output size and blocked preservation edits. The existing 11 import integration groups also continue to pass.
- [Exporter regression](../../packages/pptx-exporter/test/chart-transforms.test.mjs): all current chart families retain transforms, including minimal identity transforms.
- [Chromium](../../scripts/browser-bundles.test.mjs): full normalized documents/reports, fresh/no-op bytes and chart edits/reimport agree with Node, with no network requests. [Isolated installed consumers](../../tests/consumers/node.mjs) exercise all three families using public package entries and declarations.
- [Native experiment](../../scripts/inspect-chart-import.mjs) and [evidence](../testing/chart-import-evidence.json): LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 produces three chart families, independent Python ZIP/XML cache data equals projected Core and fresh output, exact source no-op passes, fresh output reopens to PDF. This fixture's producer leaves cache data unchanged. Interactive Edit Data, repair-dialog observation, PowerPoint and native visual equivalence remain untested.
- [Public API](../api/chart-import.md), [typed example](../examples/chart-import.ts), architecture boundary and Roadmap/backlog links describe the delivered subset and limits.

Adversarial review:

1. **Stale or sparse data silently becoming zero:** strict finite lexical parsing, sparse-cache source-only policy and explicit cache/workbook disagreements; independent negative fixtures verify outcomes.
2. **Shared range/resource amplification:** distinct-part expanded accounting plus referenced-cell work charging; exact boundary tests distinguish one shared workbook from two distinct parts and reject repeated missing-cache expansion beyond budget.
3. **Self-confirming writer/reader tests:** independent hand-authored fixtures plus output cache/every-cell inspection and native Python inventory; native interactive editing is explicitly not inferred from them.
4. **Source report mutation or false capability identity:** detached MCE snapshots, opaque extension tests and per-operation unsupported-property/source-only identity assertions; no XML source state enters Core.
5. **Fresh export masquerading as preservation:** stale-plan and blocked-edit/rebuild checks, exact no-op bytes, three-round stale-data removal and frame-transform regression; docs distinguish each operation.

Final verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, the complete `pnpm test` chain and the optional native chart runner all exited 0. Parser has 283 cases (including 22 chart groups), Exporter 48, browser bundles 21 and import integration 13; all 26 visual/measurement cases and the installed-package, release, fixture/performance, chart authoring, composition and copy suites pass. Full portable output is retained locally at `.tmp/chart-import-full-test.log`, build output at `.tmp/chart-import-build.log`. Full roadmap implementation, complete AUT-06.2 semantics, inheritance, table styles, preservation closure/comparison/edit-save, SmartArt, media, animation and remaining backlog work are unfinished. No parent/child checkbox is closed by this bounded evidence alone.

## Source placeholder matching and dimensions — 2026-10-25

Scope: partial evidence toward IMP-05.1.1/05.1.2. The full objective remains active; AUT-07/IMP-05 and their children remain unchecked.

The [placeholder profile](../api/placeholder-inheritance.md) now matches slide → layout by numeric index and layout → master by a documented type map, within the verified source relationship chain. Default/explicit type/index and parent match status are visible in `ImportReport.placeholderInheritance`. Duplicate candidates report ambiguity and a count without first-candidate guessing or per-child candidate-list expansion. A grouped first candidate cannot change the classification of an ambiguous set.

Offsets/extents retain property-level EMUs, source part/path/object identity and source level. Local zero/negative offsets and explicit false/zero transforms remain values. Supported dimensions/preset names become local Core values; master/layout prompt text is not copied. Unsupported local/custom preset choices block older inherited presets. Complete local transforms preserve existing behavior; inherited non-neutral ancestor rotation/reflection and grouped inheritance stay explicitly limited pending native evidence.

Parser reuses retained effective MCE XML, per-part path/index caches and once-resolved parent graphs. The optional `maxPlaceholderEntries` default is 100,000 distinct declarations across visited parts, counting shared layouts/masters once. Existing archive/XML/report limits and cooperative cancellation remain in effect. Source location types have a shared private owner, avoiding an import-report/inheritance-type dependency cycle. Core/Canonical IR and package dependencies are unchanged; no source template or raw XML leaks into Core.

Delivered evidence:

- [16 focused groups](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/placeholder-inheritance.test.mjs), including both dialects, aliases/effective MCE/opaque extensions, every supported master type family, default and lexical numeric indices, multiple masters, duplicate indices/types, candidate-order independence, missing relationships, ordinary shapes, explicit clearing values, grouped/rotated ancestors, malformed inputs, resource budgets, cancellation, session isolation and exact no-op/stale-plan behavior.
- [Two cross-package groups](../../scripts/placeholder-import-parity.test.mjs): three fresh rounds, independent output slide XML, exact coordinates, stable resource size, no duplicate prompts, local edits and unaffected shared-master pages. Fresh output materializes local values; it does not recreate source bindings.
- [Chromium parity](../../scripts/browser-bundles.test.mjs): Strict-source Node/browser documents, provenance, output/no-op bytes, rendered text and declaration-budget failures agree without network requests.
- [Installed Node consumer](../../tests/consumers/node.mjs) and [NodeNext declarations](../../tests/consumers/types.ts): packed public entries, source dimension provenance, fresh export/import and readonly report typing.
- [ADR 0003](../architecture/decisions/0003-placeholder-dimension-projection.md), public API/checked example, capability matrix and Roadmap/TODO/work-queue updates record the limits and next gates.

Adversarial review:

1. **Wrong source parent:** matching uses normalized numeric indices/scoped type maps and actual source relationships. Reordered candidates, lexical index aliases, duplicate identities and two-master fixtures prove no name/order/cross-master fallback.
2. **Hidden data duplication/loss:** master prompts never enter slide contents; three-round output independently contains one shape per page with unchanged EMUs/resource size. Exact source no-op is tested separately; edit/rebuild save modes remain blocked.
3. **Transform/override fabrication:** local zero/false values win, unsupported/custom geometry stops inherited preset selection, and grouped/non-neutral ancestor transforms do not gain unverified inheritance support. Tests cover each refusal and partial local transform behavior.
4. **Report/resource amplification:** source declarations and parent resolution are cached once per part; ambiguity carries count/key rather than repeated lists. Budget-boundary, captured-option, cancellation and independent-session tests pass.
5. **Premature completion:** native PowerPoint/LibreOffice template interaction/layout fidelity, theme/font/text/paint/background inheritance, Core multi-master authoring, dynamic rebinding and preservation writes remain untested or unimplemented. This evidence closes no parent/child task.

Final verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the full `pnpm test` chain exited 0 on the final runtime implementation. Parser passes 299 cases (including 16 placeholder groups), browser bundles 22, import integration 15, visual/measurement 26; Core 67, Layout 126, Exporter 48, SVG 19 and CLI 1 also pass, alongside release, dev-app, fixture/performance, installed-package, chart-authoring, composition and copy suites. The final targeted placeholder rerun additionally checks exact ancestor preset locations, absent Core IDs for omitted drawings and full-local false/zero overrides over rotated/flipped ancestors. `git diff --check` and explicit whitespace/newline checks for all nine new files pass. Logs are retained locally at `.tmp/placeholder-build-final.log`, `.tmp/placeholder-typecheck-final.log`, `.tmp/placeholder-lint-final.log` and `.tmp/placeholder-full-test-final.log`. Native template/layout results remain untested.

## Text property inheritance — 2026-10-25

Scope: a bounded Parser projection toward IMP-05.1.2. It does not close IMP-05, AUT-07 or any preservation/edit-save parent.

The new [text style profile](../api/text-style-inheritance.md) interprets supported frame margins/alignment/wrap/auto-fit and property-level run/paragraph/list styles for drawing text. It follows the matched source ancestry, selects one exact paragraph level, records selected mapped declarations and unsupported blockers in `ImportReport.textStyleInheritance`, and materializes effective values as local Core styles. Unsupported high-priority values block lower inherited values for that property. Ancestor prompt paragraphs/runs are never copied. Notes and table cells share local property/list parsing but do not receive drawing ancestry cascade or target provenance. Theme slots/modifiers, script/theme font resolution, full Office layout, background inheritance, dynamic template rebinding and edit/rebuild saves remain out of scope.

The [capability TODO](advanced-capability-todos.md) and [next-delivery queue](next-delivery-plan.md) now distinguish this delivered subset from the still-open parent contract. Public API documentation, a typed example, ADR 0004, import session limit documentation and the capability matrix describe source/target provenance, precise precedence, unsupported behavior and limitations.

Delivered evidence:

- [18 parser groups](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/text-inheritance.test.mjs): both XML dialects, nine list levels, character and automatic numbering, local zero/false clearing, precedence, unknown high-priority blockers, named master styles, plain-shape defaults, ambiguous/multiple parents, prompt exclusion, source paths, shared-source audits, notes/table local behavior, limits, cancellation and session isolation.
- [Three cross-package groups](../../scripts/text-inheritance-parity.test.mjs): independent output XML checks over three fresh rounds, stable semantics/package size, prompt exclusion, exact no-op bytes, edits isolated to one of two shared-template slides, blocked source edit/rebuild, and provenance budget failure.
- [Packed Node and Chromium consumers](../../scripts/installed-packages.test.mjs) exercise the public parser report with no network access; [NodeNext declarations](../../tests/consumers/types.ts) compile readonly provenance types and the new session limit.
- Full parser suite: 316 cases pass. Full build, typecheck, lint/docs/boundary checks and `pnpm test` pass. Logs: `.tmp/text-inheritance-build.log`, `.tmp/text-inheritance-typecheck.log`, `.tmp/text-inheritance-lint.log`, `.tmp/text-inheritance-packages-rerun.log`, `.tmp/text-inheritance-import.log`, `.tmp/text-inheritance-full-test.log`.

Adversarial review:

1. **False fallback through unsupported source values:** explicit unsupported declarations replace lower mapped declarations for the same field. Tests cover theme color, color modifiers/opacity, absent/theme fonts, invalid alignment/spacing/bullets and supported higher overrides.
2. **Wrong source scope or prompt duplication:** only the matched relationship chain contributes list/frame ancestry; multiple masters remain isolated, ambiguous placeholder candidates do not contribute placeholder styles, and prompt paragraphs never become local content. Tests cover each boundary.
3. **Incorrect clearing/default handling:** local zero, false, no-bullet, no-underline and explicit wrap/auto-fit choices are asserted independently; empty paragraphs/end properties do not restyle preceding runs.
4. **Unbounded report growth or runtime divergence:** selected property records have their own default budget and boundary failure; shared source nodes are cached. Node, browser and packed public consumers agree on effective values and provenance fields.
5. **Premature Office-fidelity claim:** output is checked independently at XML level and through repeated fresh exports, but native PowerPoint/LibreOffice layout/reflow and template editing have not been tested. The profile and parent TODO remain explicitly bounded/open.

Final verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, and the complete `pnpm test` chain exit 0. Parser: 317; browser bundles: 22; import integration: 18; Core: 67; Layout: 126; Exporter: 48; SVG: 19; the remaining visual/measurement, release, fixture/performance, installed-package, CLI, chart, composition and copy suites also pass. `git diff --check` passes. No native text rendering/editor fidelity claim is made.

## Theme color and Latin font projection — 2026-10-25

Scope: bounded implementation evidence toward IMP-05.1.3.1/.3.2 and the effective-color portion of IMP-08.1.1. The complete objective and all parent/child closure gates remain open; this is not a general package comparator, Core multi-master authoring or preservation edit-save.

The Parser resolves direct RGB/system fallback theme slots, target-scope color maps and major/minor Latin font references with `resolvedFrom` paths. Color and font scheme selection is independent across slide, layout and master overrides, followed by the verified master theme. A missing component inherits; a present component replaces its older scheme as a unit, so unsupported or missing slots block older values. Local and inherited declarations use the same target scope. Component selection is cached per scope; shared partial override parts cannot combine the inherited components of different masters. Theme/override content types, roots and relationship modes are validated.

The previous table/reference parity failures compared source theme roles with projected RGB strings. Independent source theme and master-map XML establish the expected effective colors. The fresh-export comparison now resolves only exact theme-color fields against each document's palette and still compares all other fields. New negative cases detect wrong colors and unrelated style changes; source no-op continues to compare exact bytes. This helper is limited to these tests and does not complete IMP-08's general comparison contract.

Delivered evidence:

- [Text/theme parser tests](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/text-inheritance.test.mjs) and [eight partial-override groups](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/theme-overrides.test.mjs): both dialects; slide/layout/master color-only, font-only and empty overrides; local/inherited source declarations; shared partial components across two masters; unknown/missing higher slots; effective MCE; wrong content types/roots/namespaces, external/duplicate relationships and duplicate schemes; no-op and session isolation.
- [Fresh-export color checks](../../scripts/import-content-parity.test.mjs): custom nondefault palettes, equality/inequality and unrelated-style counterexamples, independent exported XML, three rounds, table/ref geometry and source-byte preservation.
- [Three-round text integration](../../scripts/text-inheritance-parity.test.mjs): layout font-only override inherits the base color scheme; output XML, concrete font/color values, provenance, local edits and package size remain stable.
- [Chromium/Node global bundle parity](../../scripts/browser-bundles.test.mjs) checks Strict input, complete documents/reports, no-op/fresh bytes, reimport and invalid override rejection with no network requests.
- [Installed consumers](../../scripts/installed-packages.test.mjs) now use a partial layout font override; packed Node/browser entries and public declarations verify effective font/color and source dependencies.
- [Optional native runner](../../scripts/inspect-theme-text-import.mjs) and [recorded evidence](../testing/theme-text-import-evidence.json) independently inspect actual native PDF spans. LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 renders the source's base color/font, while fresh output renders the selected override values. Native save changes the projected run styles. All style-parity flags are recorded as false; successful processing is not counted as fidelity. PowerPoint 16.104 open/export timed out; Computer Use confirmed the Mac was locked, so its rendering/repair/editing checks remain untested.

Adversarial review:

1. **False equality after deleting color assertions:** exact known-role normalization plus custom palettes, wrong-color and changed-bold counterexamples; independent theme/map/source XML and explicit projected RGB checks remain.
2. **Partial override destroys the unrelated scheme:** color/font components are selected independently; color-only, font-only and empty override cases cover all three source scopes and both dialects.
3. **Stale or cross-master fallback:** present unsupported slots remain blockers; two masters share a partial override while retaining different base colors/fonts, with per-target provenance.
4. **Invalid relationships or hidden network loading:** content-type/root/dialect, duplicate/scheme and external-target failures are asserted through public import errors; Chromium records zero requests. Existing archive/XML/report budgets and cooperative checkpoints remain in force.
5. **Automated tests become a native-fidelity claim:** the native PDF/producer experiment records its actual differences without changing Parser expectations to make them disappear. No native visual/template guarantee or task-completion checkbox is added.

Final portable verification on 2026-10-25: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain exited 0. Parser: **331**; browser bundles: **23**; import integration: **19**; Core: 67; Layout: 126; Exporter: 48; SVG: 19; visual/measurement: 26. Installed-package, declaration, release, fixture/performance, chart, composition, copy, CLI and dev-app suites pass. Logs: `.tmp/theme-final-build.log`, `.tmp/theme-final-typecheck.log`, `.tmp/theme-final-lint.log`, `.tmp/theme-final-full-test.log`. The optional native experiment completed its inventory with explicit style differences; log: `.tmp/theme-text-native.log`.
