## Ordered text color transforms and alpha — 2026-10-25

Tasks: **IMP-05.1.3.3.1**, with shared Core/Renderer/Exporter text-style support. This is implementation progress within that task; its inherited gates, complete native fidelity and preservation writes remain open.

The [color profile](../api/text-color-transforms.md) now evaluates eleven named transforms: tint/shade, luminance and saturation set/mod/off, and alpha set/mod/off. Direct sRGB, portable system fallback and scoped theme palette/reference chains use source-order evaluation. The pure Parser evaluator retains floating RGB/HSL/linear state until final quantization; consecutive HSL operations retain hue/saturation at gray/black intermediate states. Unknown, malformed, nested and foreign transforms block the whole color declaration rather than yielding a partial result.

Core adds optional finite `colorOpacity` for runs. Explicit opaque fills reset inherited alpha; unsupported fills block RGB and alpha together. Normalized IR v2 omits opaque alpha, preserving existing opaque snapshots. Text/shape/table/notes output combines run alpha with existing element opacity once; native SVG and mixed-run HTML apply the same value. Transparent text retains its content and layout. HTML list markers follow the first run's alpha, and empty notes with only nonopaque styling remain authored content.

Palette/map/transform paths remain Parser-owned; no XML or source handles enter Core. A separate captured `maxTextStyleDependencies` session budget, default 1,000,000 locations, bounds repeated source dependencies independently from the selected-property count. Shared long-transform fixtures verify that many targets cannot bypass the budget.

### Verification

- [Core tests](../../packages/core/test/text-opacity.test.mjs): four new groups for immutable snapshots, duplication, preset/reset rules, shape/table/notes paths, invalid alpha and old IR shape.
- [Parser tests](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/color-transforms.test.mjs): seventeen new groups covering independent HSL constants (also checked with Python `colorsys`), linear tint/shade expectations, ordered/clamped arithmetic, alpha composition, integer/percent lexical rules, XML whitespace, unknown/nested/foreign inputs, all 256 gray identity values, both dialects, transformed partial overrides, provenance, exact no-op, budget boundaries/options capture and shared-source amplification. Existing blocker tests now use genuinely unsupported transforms and retain positive assertions for newly mapped values.
- [Cross-package tests](../../scripts/text-inheritance-parity.test.mjs): three new groups verify source transforms are materialized once, independent slide/notes alpha XML, element/run alpha multiplication, all local text owners and three fresh rounds without cumulative color changes or package growth.
- [Exporter regression](../../packages/pptx-exporter/test/notes.test.mjs): empty notes with only alpha are exported; ordinary opaque empty notes retain previous omission behavior.
- [Chromium measurement/visual suite](../../packages/svg-renderer/test/measurement.spec.mjs): a new group checks native SVG pixel minima independently, mixed-run computed alpha, transparent list markers and unchanged run widths. All 27 visual/measurement tests pass after the final marker fix.
- [Installed Node](../../tests/consumers/node.mjs) and [declaration](../../tests/consumers/types.ts) consumers exercise public alpha authoring/preview/import and the new optional budget without workspace-private paths.
- [Native experiment](../../scripts/inspect-color-transform-import.mjs) and [evidence](../testing/color-transform-import-evidence.json): nine direct samples, exact original-byte no-op, isolated LibreOfficeDev producer save, independent PDF text/raster inspection, source/output hashes and observed per-case agreement. Eight samples match saved styles and source/fresh rasters; gray shade yields native `5E5E5E` versus the IEC profile's `5C5C5C`. The discrepancy is retained, not turned into a passing fidelity assertion.

The complete `pnpm test` chain passed: Core **71**, Layout **126**, Parser **348**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, visual/measurement **27**, browser bundles **23**, import integration **22**, plus release, installed package/type, fixture/performance, chart, composition, copy, CLI and dev-app checks. This adds 26 test groups across the portable suites. Final workspace build and typecheck pass; the final visual rerun includes the list-marker change. Logs: `.tmp/color-transforms-final-full-test.log`, `.tmp/color-transforms-final-build.log`, `.tmp/color-transforms-final-typecheck.log`, `.tmp/color-transforms-final-visual.log`; native log: `.tmp/color-transforms-native.log`.

### Adversarial review and remaining scope

1. **False native equivalence:** keep the gray-shade source/native/fresh differences in the evidence. A headless conversion or eight matching cases cannot establish PowerPoint, interactive editing, all transforms or cross-application parity.
2. **Inherited-alpha leakage:** a new opaque fill supplies alpha 1; an unsupported fill blocks both RGB and alpha. Tests verify that older translucent colors cannot survive a higher unsupported declaration.
3. **Order/precision and repeated application:** retain HSL state through achromatic intermediates, keep palette/reference transforms in one evaluation chain, quantize RGB only at the end, and materialize effective output once. Independent constants and three-round checks verify the outcome.
4. **Hidden loss or identity changes:** transparent text remains present, notes retain explicit styling, no-op bytes retain original transform expressions, and source edit/rebuild stays blocked. Fresh export does not claim to reconstruct expression or template identity.
5. **Resource/diagnostic failures:** count dependency occurrences independently from property records; avoid spreading unbounded source arrays into function arguments; report unknown transform reasons at the color while still locating unconsumed transform subtrees. Long shared sources and budget/invalid-source cases verify controlled failure.

IMP-05.1.3.3.1 remains unchecked for its complete inherited/native/preservation requirements. Non-Latin fonts, backgrounds/master artwork, multi-master authoring/rebinding, preservation closure/comparison/edit-save, SmartArt, media, animations and the rest of the original backlog remain unfinished. The full thread goal is still active.

## Independent OPC source comparison — 2026-10-25

Task scope: concrete progress on **IMP-08.1.1–.1.3**, which remain unchecked for their full semantic/native requirements. This follows the planning turn that added 27 child tasks; it is implementation work toward the original full-backlog goal.

### Implementation and contract

- Public `compareOpcPackages` in Parser root, browser ESM and global exports. Default `opc-payload-v1` compares exact expanded part/control payloads while allowing ZIP container representation differences. Explicit `opc-xml-v1` permits only documented XML representation differences.
- Compare exact part/content-type identities and source-local relationship keys, raw/resolved targets, fragments and external targets. Every unreferenced source part participates; binary comparison uses actual bytes, including equal-length CRC32 collisions.
- Private XML document retention now includes prolog/epilog, comments, processing instructions and declaration standalone state alongside the existing root. The conservative token walker retains namespaces, prefixes, whitespace, order, opaque extensions and unselected MCE branches; it is iterative.
- Snapshot both buffers and options before yielding. Bound combined decompression, comparison work and retained findings independently; validate both candidates even for identical inputs or truncated reports. Abort/failure returns no partial comparison or output mutation.
- No new package/dependency, Core/IR field, Exporter dependency, network retrieval, writer or preservation-handle coupling. [ADR 0005](../architecture/decisions/0005-opc-source-comparison.md), [API profile](../api/package-comparison.md), checked example, package docs, capability matrix and planning entries record the boundary.

### Verification

Added **26 test groups**: 22 [Parser groups](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/comparison.test.mjs), three [cross-package groups](../../scripts/package-comparison-parity.test.mjs), and one ESM/global [browser group](../../scripts/browser-bundles.test.mjs). Existing installed Node/declaration consumers now exercise the public API and readonly profile/report contracts.

Independent expectations include a handwritten ZIP fixture writer, a separate ZIP reader plus Node SHA256, Chromium DOM structure, fixed binary CRC collision values, and single-fault candidate mutations. Coverage includes alpha/notes/unknown XML/workbook-byte changes, missing resources, relationship identity/target changes, namespaces/QName bindings, CDATA/encoding/attribute-order rules, prolog/epilog loss, both-buffer capture, report truncation, exact budgets, cancellation and deep traversal. Three reopened no-op rounds retain every original payload without growth; fresh export's loss of injected opaque source content is detected.

Full `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` passed. Test totals include Core **71**, Layout **126**, Parser **370**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, visual/measurement **27**, browser bundles **24**, import integration **25**, release **16**, installed packages **5**, fixture/performance **12**, composition **10**, copy **3**, chart parity **1**, CLI **1** and dev-app **16**, all with zero failures. Logs: `.tmp/comparison-full-build.log`, `.tmp/comparison-full-typecheck.log`, `.tmp/comparison-lint.log`, `.tmp/comparison-full-test.log`; earlier targeted logs: `.tmp/comparison-targeted.log`, `.tmp/comparison-parity.log`, `.tmp/comparison-browser.log`.

[Retained-native artifact evidence](../testing/package-comparison-evidence.json) rechecks source/native SHA256 against the previous LibreOfficeDev color experiment. Python `zipfile` independently confirms the payload profile's one added part, 23 removed parts and 30 changed payloads; both comparison profiles report 102 category findings. The [standalone runner](../../scripts/inspect-package-comparison.mjs) emits both profiles and hashes for any input pair. This was reinspection of existing artifacts, with no new native application launch, editing/playback claim or assumption that all producer rewrites are data loss.

### Adversarial review and remaining scope

1. **False equality:** no Core projection, CRC shortcut or ignored unknown/MCE branch can erase source differences. Fixed CRC collisions, opaque content and independent readers verify this.
2. **Unsafe XML normalization:** retain lexical prefixes, namespace bindings, text spaces, order, comments, processing instructions and standalone state; explicit profile tests and DOM checks cover permitted/forbidden differences.
3. **Failure bypass:** validate malformed identical inputs and the complete candidate before returning; truncation affects only retained findings. Broken resources/duplicate relationship IDs reject, and work/expansion exhaustion has no partial result.
4. **Mutation or resource coupling:** snapshot both inputs/options, keep the signal live, freeze returned reports and avoid global/session/writer state. Cancellation and independent retry are tested.
5. **Overstated completion:** source equality does not establish full OOXML validity, effective-value fresh equivalence, native editing/playback or an edit-save implementation. Full identity bijections, named digest reporting, object/workbook semantic corruption and application reopening gates remain pending under IMP-08; IMP-09 and the full goal remain active.

## Source relationship preservation closure — 2026-10-25

Concrete progress on **IMP-07.1.1**, which remains unchecked for full preservation-state retention, implicit reference boundaries, reconciliation and operation-level native evidence.

Public `inspectOpcClosure` is available in Parser root/browser/global exports. The `source-relationships-v1` profile follows every recognized raw source relationship across inactive MCE branches, includes owned relationship parts, and inventories outside incoming owners and unreached parts. Shared, self and cyclic targets are visited once. Explicit roots use OPC identity rules and deterministic provenance. External targets are never retrieved; embedded package bytes are not interpreted recursively.

Incomplete/opaque source declarations, missing inactive targets and potentially coexisting relationship IDs produce located findings. Mutually exclusive branches of the same AlternateContent do not cause false identity collisions. Directional coverage describes this source relationship profile only; unreached parts are never declared disposable. Private relationship-only tree retention avoids keeping ordinary XML trees for this analysis. Root/raw-declaration/selected-byte/finding/work budgets, snapshots and cancellation are explicit; exhausted findings throw rather than returning a misleading complete graph.

Added **28 test groups**: 24 [Parser groups](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/closure.test.mjs), three [integration groups](../../scripts/preservation-closure-parity.test.mjs), and one ESM/global [browser group](../../scripts/browser-bundles.test.mjs). Independent expectations include 32 randomized graph-model cases, a separate ZIP reader/SHA256, Chromium DOM, 300-node cycles and 300-level inactive XML. Tests cover generated chart/workbook/notes/image packages, outside shared owners, inactive failures, exact limits, input/options capture, deep freezing and no-op preservation. Installed Node/declaration consumers exercise the public surface.

The [retained-artifact evidence](../testing/preservation-closure-evidence.json) verifies source/native SHA256 against the earlier LibreOfficeDev color experiment. Independent Python ZIP/XML traversal agrees for all four root/artifact combinations. Package roots select 52/30 parts; first-slide roots select 12/7 parts, with 20/11 outside incoming declarations and 40/23 unreached parts respectively. This is reinspection without a new application launch or native editing/playback claim. The [runner](../../scripts/inspect-preservation-closure.mjs) is read-only and emits source hashes.

Full `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` exited 0. Core **71**, Layout **126**, Parser **394**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, visual/measurement **27**, browser bundles **25**, import integration **28**, release **16**, installed packages **5**, fixture/performance **12**, composition **10**, copy **3**, chart parity **1**, CLI **1** and dev-app suites pass. Logs: `.tmp/closure-full-build.log`, `.tmp/closure-full-typecheck.log`, `.tmp/closure-lint.log`, `.tmp/closure-full-test.log`; final focused Parser/integration log: `.tmp/closure-targeted-final.log` (**27** groups).

Adversarial review:

1. **Inactive dependency loss:** traverse raw branches, including parseable targets whose inactive ID/type is invalid; source counts and hidden missing-target counterexamples are independent of effective edges.
2. **Mistaken cleanup authority:** outside owners and all unreached parts remain visible; no-op retains original payloads and edit/rebuild remain blocked. No package extraction or garbage collection is added.
3. **False identity completeness:** record branch scopes; same-AlternateContent alternatives are exclusive, unconditional/separate scopes remain conservatively coexistent. Value matching is not presented as selected source-node identity.
4. **Budget/snapshot bypass:** charge global raw declarations and work, selected unique byte footprint and all findings; immutable inputs/options, cancellation, exact-boundary and independent retry tests cover failures.
5. **Overstated fidelity:** complete relationship coverage does not prove arbitrary XML/binary reference coverage, safe subtree edits, native save or full backlog completion. Mixed XML modification boundaries and scoped edit-save are next; the original full goal remains active.

## Lexical XML character-edit foundation — 2026-10-25

Concrete progress within **IMP-07.2.1**, not completion of that task or IMP-09. [ADR 0007](../architecture/decisions/0007-xml-character-edit-boundaries.md) records the private transformation and the remaining source-to-Core, package and application-level gates.

Parser now has a private [XML text source](../../packages/pptx-parser/src/preservation/xml-text-source.ts) that validates captured source XML, binds complete raw element preorder to lexical ranges and replaces explicitly identified character-only targets. Guards include expanded namespace/local identity, exact baseline text, unique targets and disjoint source ranges. Text/CDATA/empty targets are supported; child elements, comments and processing instructions inside a target reject atomically. Equal-text requests retain original representation.

The transform copies unselected lexical regions verbatim and retains UTF-8 or BOM-marked UTF-16 byte order. It escapes delimiters and carriage returns, rejects invalid XML characters/lone surrogates, and reparses the candidate under original XML limits before returning. Request/replacement/encoded-output/work budgets are independent; byte accounting includes BOM and entity expansion before output allocation. Invocation-time byte/options/edit capture, detached output, immutable metadata, per-candidate original baselines, cancellation, disposal and independent failure retry are covered. No package/dependency, public API export, Core XML field, production ZIP writer or edit-save permission was added.

### Verification

Added **23 groups**: 22 [Parser groups](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/xml-text-source.test.mjs) and one test-only private bundle [Chromium group](../../scripts/browser-bundles.test.mjs).

- Explicit raw-string and independent Buffer encodings check UTF-8 BOM, UTF-16LE/BE, Unicode qualified names, CR/CRLF normalization, CDATA/entities, self-closing/empty targets, quoted delimiters, repeated text and namespace shadowing. Every unselected lexical region is compared, including unknown effects/extensions and inactive MCE branches.
- Sixty-four independently assembled template cases vary target indices and source encodings. Injection, mixed child content, identity/baseline conflicts and duplicate targets have rejecting counterexamples.
- Exact edit/replacement/encoded-byte/work budgets, original XML revalidation budgets, mutable caller bytes/options/edits, deeply frozen metadata, pre/in-flight cancellation, getter-reentrant disposal, many small elements and deep nesting are checked.
- Three fixture patch/repack/reimport rounds in **both** Strict and Transitional compare untouched parts through a separate ZIP reader/SHA256. Source IDs, unknown effect/extension payloads, relationship/incoming/unreached inventories and package part counts remain stable. No-op retains each candidate byte-exactly and session edit mode still rejects.
- Chromium independently parses candidate bytes with DOMParser and compares text, namespace-qualified identities and opaque values; all three encoded outputs match separate Node Buffer expectations. Budget/disposal failures stay controlled and no network requests occur. The private module is bundled only for the test.

Full `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` passed. Parser **416**, Core **71**, Layout **126**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, visual/measurement **27**, import integration **28**, release **16**, installed packages **5**, fixture/performance **12**, composition **10**, copy **3**, chart parity **1**, CLI **1**, dev-app **16**, with no failed/skipped tests. The full chain included browser **25**; after adding the independent private DOM case, final `pnpm test:browser-bundles` passed **26**, zero failures/skips. Logs: `.tmp/xml-text-full-build.log`, `.tmp/xml-text-full-typecheck.log`, `.tmp/xml-text-lint.log`, `.tmp/xml-text-full-test.log`, `.tmp/xml-text-final-browser.log`; focused tests: `.tmp/xml-text-targeted.log` (**22**).

### Adversarial review and next work

1. **Editing the wrong repeated string:** locate by original element index and expanded identity, then compare baseline text; no global search/replacement or effective-tree-only serialization is used. Repeated text, namespace shadowing and raw wrapper indices have counterexamples.
2. **Losing opaque neighbors through serialization:** copy original lexical regions and source encoding; independent exact byte expectations cover BOM, comments, PI, extensions, namespace/QName scope, CDATA outside the target and inactive branches.
3. **Injection or changed text after parsing:** escape markup and CR, validate XML 1.0 code points, reparse and compare requested text/element identities; the independent browser parser agrees. Mixed children/comments/PI inside a target reject rather than disappearing.
4. **Partial state on cancellation, exhaustion or disposal:** no source baseline mutates; output budgets are charged before allocation, each candidate remains private until validated, and lifecycle checks catch getter reentrancy and in-flight disposal. Independent retry remains valid.
5. **Mistaking a part transform for preservation save:** production ZIP writing, source-to-Core run ownership, modification classification, text fields/animation ranges, shared source parts, signed-package policy, save-plan integration and native reopen/edit evidence remain pending. Test fixture repacking does not establish a writer contract.

Final lint/boundary/documentation checks also passed after the execution record and planning updates (`.tmp/xml-text-final-lint.log`). Tracked diffs and the three new primitive/test/ADR files pass Git whitespace checks.

IMP-07.2.1 and IMP-09 stay unchecked. Next implementation binds imported text runs to allowed raw source targets, classifies changes/restores and defines affected reference ownership before connecting bounded package candidates to prepared session plans. SmartArt, media, animation and the rest of the original full-backlog objective remain active.

## Direct text-run preservation saves (2026-10-25)

Concrete implementation and verification progress on **IMP-07.2.1, IMP-09.1.1–09.1.3, IMP-09.2.1/09.2.1.1 and IMP-09.5.1/09.5.2**. [ADR 0008](../architecture/decisions/0008-direct-text-preservation-save.md) and the [checked public contract](../api/text-edit-save.md) define the experimental profile. The previous primitive-only record above describes its earlier state; production package/session wiring now exists. Complete parent and inherited/native gates remain open.

### Implemented behavior and ownership

- `importPptx(source, { projection: "content", editSaveProfile: "text-runs-v1" })` explicitly enables existing bound text-box run character edits, including mapped nested groups. Default imports keep edit blocking; invalid profile/structure combinations reject. Shape/table/notes/chart text, styles, geometry, assets, topology, sections and metadata are not admitted.
- Private MCE origin maps connect effective copied nodes to original raw preorder. Public readonly `textRuns` expose bound/unmapped locations without XML/source payload getters; Parser retains baseline characters and namespace ownership privately. No source XML or handle enters Core/IR, and no package or Exporter dependency is added.
- Whole normalized-snapshot analysis produces a complete bounded `changes` report, classifies unsupported mutation kinds, excludes only actual text owners' derived `plainText`, and blocks mixed plans atomically. Shared source atoms must agree. Raw guards include inactive branches, normalized drawing target IDs, text/sub-object targets and signature/VBA declarations; incomplete relationship closure blocks.
- Lexical XML replacement retains surrounding encodings/namespaces/order/unknown content. The private ZIP32 writer validates the original archive first, retains untouched compressed local records and central metadata, and rebases offsets with new CRC/sizes for changed entries. It deletes no parts and rewrites no identities or relationships.
- Candidate acceptance requires exact OPC differences restricted to approved XML payloads, followed by reprojection of the actual output and equality with the entire planned Core snapshot. No-op retains exact original bytes, saves do not advance baselines, and all ready profiles remain loss-free. Nonempty loss consent and rebuild stay blocked.
- New independent analysis/change/validation budgets complement captured XML/ZIP/OPC limits. Edit preparation reserves the maximum output allowance before work and shrinks the reservation when ready. A per-operation controller propagates abort/disposal; one caller-signal relay serves concurrent preparations.

### Adversarial finding and correction

A new regression cancels or disposes **after** child subscription, proving the operation has passed the initial snapshot yield and acquired its output reservation. Before the correction, synchronous disposal left one listener on the caller signal until the async operation unwound: `.tmp/text-save-active-disposal-before.log` records the failing `1 !== 0` assertion.

Session disposal now aborts child controllers and synchronously removes/clears caller relay subscriptions. Normal operation finalizers can unsubscribe again safely. The regression verifies immediate listener removal, typed cancellation/disposal, unchanged source bytes, and successful repeated retries under one-plan/full-output reservation limits. It passes after rebuilding: `.tmp/text-save-active-disposal-after.log` contains **53** text/session cases. Public Chromium ESM/global coverage independently observes relay installation/removal and the disposed result; it also verifies Node/browser candidate bytes, report/state and raw DOM identities.

### Verification and native evidence

Added **31 groups** across the [19 text-save cases](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/text-save.test.mjs), [8 archive-replacement cases](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/archive-replacement.test.mjs), [3 cross-package cases](../../scripts/text-save-parity.test.mjs) and one [public browser group](../../scripts/browser-bundles.test.mjs). Installed Node/types consumers exercise the real public profile.

Coverage includes both dialects, namespace aliases and MCE raw origins; repeated/empty/CDATA/mixed text; XML injection/CR/UTF-16BE; exact unknown/unreached payload retention; shared images/chart-workbooks/tables/notes/grouped text; known whole-shape versus range targets; unsupported mixed plans; invalid characters and controlled retries; stale/foreign/disposed plans and exact consent; full budget failures, reservation shrinkage, shared caller signals and at least three save/reimport rounds.

Final **`pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm test` pass** after the disposal fix. The complete run records Parser **443**, Core **71**, Layout **126**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, browser bundles **27**, visual/measurement **27**, import integration **31**, release **16**, installed-package **5**, fixtures/performance **12**, composition **10**, copy **3**, chart parity **1**, CLI **1** and dev-app **16**, with zero failed/skipped tests. Logs: `.tmp/text-save-final-build.log`, `.tmp/text-save-final-typecheck.log`, `.tmp/text-save-final-test.log`. Initial focused verification passed **63** cases before the new regression; its log is `.tmp/text-save-current-targeted.log`.

The [native experiment](../../scripts/inspect-text-edit-save.mjs) and retained [evidence](../testing/text-edit-save-evidence.json) use **LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 2c87e51eeaa2b413ff4ae097b2705eea1995d8e5**. Independent Python ZIP/XML confirms only `ppt/slides/slide1.xml` changes and all source parts survive in the PPTKit candidate. Native headless resave and PDF text inspection retain `Edited preserved text 123 <&>` and the unchanged control. Native resave loses the unknown extension and orphan; those losses are recorded rather than treated as fidelity passes. This turn rechecked all three retained artifact SHA256 values, the part set, selected payload difference and XML text against the evidence. Regenerating the edit with the final implementation reproduces the retained candidate byte-for-byte and report-for-report. The output algorithm was unchanged by the disposal cleanup; the native application was not rerun for that lifecycle-only fix.

### Final adversarial review and remaining scope

1. **Wrong source target or unsafe text range:** raw origins/preorder/expanded identities and expected baseline characters replace string matching; inactive range targets and shared-source conflicts block. Arbitrary opaque reference semantics are not claimed.
2. **Rebuilding away unknown content:** complete input validation precedes raw ZIP reuse, untouched records/payloads retain identity, and exact comparison plus candidate reprojection reject unapproved differences. Independent byte/ZIP/XML/DOM evidence supplements the production reader.
3. **Partial commit or resource leakage:** unsupported mixed plans return no output; disposal/cancellation release reservations, immediately detach caller listeners and allow valid independent retries. The new regression first failed and then passed.
4. **False general support:** other owners/styles/topology/assets/structural edits and all rebuild requests stay blocked. Default behavior remains compatible. API, README, architecture, roadmap and planning entries now distinguish this explicit profile from broader work.
5. **Overstated native completeness:** headless text survival is not interactive editing, repair-dialog validation, PowerPoint behavior or complete appearance/unknown-feature fidelity. Known producer losses are explicit. Parent tasks remain unchecked, and the full-backlog goal remains active.

Next work expands the explicit source-edit contract by owner and operation under IMP-09.2.1.2/.1.3 and IMP-09.2.2–.2.8, while completing source-reference/native gates and the independently queued SmartArt/media/animation work. Node file replacement, loss-bearing rebuild and structural reconciliation remain separate unimplemented slices.

Final `pnpm lint` passed after the contract, architecture and task-progress updates, including package boundaries, local links and all checked TypeScript examples (`.tmp/text-save-final-lint.log`). Tracked changes pass `git diff --check`; newly added profile/source/test/ADR/example files receive a separate whitespace/conflict-marker check because untracked files are absent from Git's default diff.

## Shape-text and speaker-notes preservation saves (2026-10-25)

Concrete implementation progress on **IMP-09.2.1.2**, retaining the existing V1 text-box contract. The task and its inherited/native parent gates remain unchecked. [ADR 0009](../architecture/decisions/0009-text-owner-preservation-save.md), the [public owner contract](../api/text-owner-save.md) and its checked TypeScript example specify the additional explicit `text-runs-v2` profile.

### Implementation and verification scope

V2 binds existing mapped shape-text and direct notes-body runs through effective-to-raw origins. Drawing provenance retains required element IDs and adds V2 owner tags. A separate immutable notes record carries slide/body/part identity, without a fabricated drawing ID. V1 records and default report shapes remain compatible. Notes bindings require one direct local body; ambiguous, missing and inherited/master-only bodies remain unbound. Shared notes parts retain the structural import rejection.

The pure snapshot analyzer maps both owner paths and admits scalar characters only. Derived plainText exemption is restricted to actual text owners; similarly named custom-data fields remain ordinary changes. Combined drawing/notes provenance uses one bounded run collection. Source guards scan raw notes parts as well as slides, including inactive branches, and scope references by part plus source object identity. Minimal XML/ZIP patches, exact candidate differences and whole-document actual-output reprojection reuse the existing pipeline.

Added **23 test groups**: [19 Parser groups](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/text-owners-save.test.mjs), [three mixed-document groups](../../scripts/text-owner-save-parity.test.mjs) and one [ESM/global browser group](../../scripts/browser-bundles.test.mjs). Installed [Node](../../tests/consumers/node.mjs) and [declaration](../../tests/consumers/types.ts) consumers exercise both owners, frozen notes provenance and absence of fabricated element IDs.

Tests cover both dialects, seven presets/painted text boxes/grouped shapes, repeated text and opaque effects, per-part links, raw MCE identity, mixed/break/field atoms, ambiguous bodies, range/whole-shape targets, aliased-source consensus and conflict, style/geometry/topology/custom-data mixed mutations, no-op restoration, stale plans, combined budgets, invalid-character retries and unlike UTF-16 byte orders. Three-round generated decks independently verify untouched chart/workbook/table/image/other-note payloads, graph identities, selected two-part differences, full normalized state and stable size. Browser bytes, reports, documents and provenance agree with Node; independent DOM checks locate each run within its own source part and no network requests occur.

The initial targeted run exposed two test assumptions: shared notes parts were already rejected by inspection, and topology changes were reported at container granularity. Tests now preserve those contracts. A further failing custom-data mutation assigned an unsupported element property that Core correctly omitted; the test now uses legal slide custom data and separately exercises a nested normalized `text.plainText` counterexample in the analyzer. No production guard was weakened to satisfy those expectations.

### Native experiment

The [runner](../../scripts/inspect-text-owner-save.mjs) and [retained evidence](../testing/text-owner-save-evidence.json) use **LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 2c87e51eeaa2b413ff4ae097b2705eea1995d8e5** with a mixed two-slide deck. Independent Python zipfile/ElementTree confirms the entire part set and every unselected payload are retained, with only `ppt/slides/slide1.xml` and `ppt/notesSlides/notesSlide1.xml` changing. Both retain injected unknown extensions; the orphan survives. The headless producer resave retains edited shape/notes characters, nested/table/second-slide text and control notes. A two-page PDF contains the edited shape and second-slide control. Native resave drops the opaque extensions/orphan; the evidence records this producer loss.

This is not PowerPoint, interactive editing, repair-dialog verification, notes-page rendering, complete appearance fidelity or arbitrary implicit-reference validation.

### Adversarial review

1. **Cross-owner ID collision:** equal source drawing ID `2` in slide and notes parts could authorize or block the wrong owner. Per-part range tests exercise both directions; only the referenced owner blocks. Notes report/change records omit element IDs.
2. **Accidental capability expansion:** V1/default and structure-projection counterexamples retain their restrictions; unsupported shape families, notes masters, tables/charts and synthetic/mixed atoms acquire no bindings. Mixed style/geometry/topology/custom-data changes block atomically.
3. **Hidden normalized mutations:** nested custom-data `text.plainText` is detected despite the legitimate derived-field exemption. Actual-output reprojection still compares the entire planned document.
4. **Round-trip data loss or resource drift:** separate ZIP/SHA256 checks, graph comparison, three rounds, unlike source encodings, combined binding limits and invalid-character/retry cases verify the selected boundary. Source baselines and other payloads remain intact.
5. **Overclaiming evidence:** native producer losses are explicit, complete inherited/native gates remain unchecked, and broader property/topology/rebuild/SmartArt/media/animation work remains open under the full active objective.

### Final checks

`pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain exited **0**. The documentation checker first caught a nullable/string notes-input guard in the new checked example and missing execution-record anchors; the example now narrows notes with `Array.isArray`, and the linked record exists. Lint then passed structure, dependency boundaries, documentation links and all checked examples.

The full run reports Parser **462**, Core **71**, Layout **126**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, browser bundles **28**, visual/measurement **27**, import integration **34**, release **16**, installed-package **5**, fixtures/performance **12**, composition **10**, copy **3**, chart parity **1**, CLI **1** and dev-app **16**, with zero failed/skipped tests. Logs: `.tmp/text-owners-full-build.log`, `.tmp/text-owners-full-typecheck.log`, `.tmp/text-owners-full-lint.log`, `.tmp/text-owners-full-test.log`. Earlier dedicated browser and package runs also passed.

The final built implementation reproduces the retained native candidate SHA256 and complete save report exactly; source, edited and native artifact hashes match the evidence (`.tmp/text-owners-native-recheck.log`). Optional native command output is `.tmp/text-owners-native.log`. This verifies retained-artifact identity without overstating the scope of the application experiment.

The full-backlog objective remains active. Next source-save work covers finer text topology, tables/charts, formatting, geometry/assets, structure/reconciliation, atomic file output and loss-bearing rebuild, alongside the independently planned SmartArt/media/animation tasks. No broad or inherited/native checkbox is closed by this owner profile.

## Table-anchor character preservation saves (2026-10-25)

Concrete implementation progress on the first text-only slice of **IMP-09.2.5**. The task and inherited/native gates remain unchecked. [ADR 0010](../architecture/decisions/0010-table-text-preservation-save.md) and the [public table-save contract](../api/table-text-save.md) define explicit `text-runs-v3`, retaining V1/V2 scope/report compatibility.

### Implementation and focused evidence

The table reader validates the complete physical grid before collecting any anchor run. Private callbacks carry the effective source cell, row, compact Core cell and physical source column. MCE origins bind exact raw character atoms; text matching and compact-index guesses are not used. Missing bodies/synthetic runs remain explicitly unmapped; unsupported tables acquire no bindings. Public immutable `tableTextRuns` retains the table's real element ID and source cell identity, without fabricated cell IDs or private source handles.

Private scalar identity now includes table row/cell coordinates. The pure analyzer maps only tables with bindings, retaining earlier profile preprocessing scope, while the full diff still rejects unsupported mutations everywhere. Scalar table column widths/row heights classify as geometry and spans as structure. Existing shared-source consensus, source-part range guards, lexical XML edits, ZIP patching, exact candidate differences, whole-output reprojection and lifecycle/budgets are reused. All owner provenance shares the existing bounded collection.

Added **20 test groups**: [17 Parser groups](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/table-save.test.mjs), [two integration groups](../../scripts/table-save-parity.test.mjs) and one [ESM/global browser group](../../scripts/browser-bundles.test.mjs). Installed [Node](../../tests/consumers/node.mjs) and [declaration](../../tests/consumers/types.ts) consumers exercise merged anchors and immutable physical coordinates.

Coverage includes both dialects, 24 independently bisected physical-grid partitions, vertical/horizontal/full-row occupancy, multiple/nested rotated tables, MCE aliases/inactive duplicates, repeated/empty/mixed/synthetic atoms, links/fields/table-style IDs/unknown extensions, raw whole-table versus range/opaque targets, styles/metrics/spans/topology/actions in mixed plans, exact undo/freshness, shared raw conflicts, combined table/notes provenance limits, complete change limits, invalid-character retries, UTF-16 and three save/reimport rounds. Generated mixed decks simultaneously modify shape, notes and two tables while retaining chart/workbook/image/other-note payloads and graph identity. Separate ZIP/SHA256 and exact difference checks supplement production reprojection.

Browser DOM independently verifies physical row/column locations from each raw atom. Browser bytes/reports/documents agree with Node, stale table plans reject and no network requests occur. The initial empty-cell test assumed every public cell input was already paragraph-shaped; missing bodies correctly expose empty string input. The test helper now respects both legal Core input representations without granting missing-body save permission.

### Native evidence

The [runner](../../scripts/inspect-table-save.mjs) and [retained evidence](../testing/table-save-evidence.json) use **LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 2c87e51eeaa2b413ff4ae097b2705eea1995d8e5**. Independent Python ZIP/ElementTree checks confirm exactly two approved text atoms differ, all non-text XML is equal, physical grids/merges and the complete part set are unchanged, and every unselected payload is byte-identical. Only `ppt/slides/slide1.xml` changes in the PPTKit candidate.

Native headless resave retains both DrawingML tables, `Anchor 123`, `Cell 456`, control cells and merge semantics. A two-page PDF contains both edits and the second-slide control. Native widths/heights change by quantization, and redundant default merge attributes appear; raw grid/attribute equality is **false** while normalized merge equality is **true**. The producer also drops opaque extension/orphan data retained by PPTKit. These are recorded producer differences, not complete fidelity passes. Interactive table editing, PowerPoint, repair dialogs and complete appearance/implicit-reference behavior remain unverified.

### Adversarial review

1. **Editing a continuation or wrong column:** compact Core index zero can correspond to physical column two. Independent partition/DOM tests and the native physical-grid inventory verify that bindings target the correct anchor and never rewrite continuations.
2. **Accidental earlier-profile expansion:** default/V1/V2 omit table provenance and reject table edits; V3 structure imports reject. Tables without safe mappings gain no bindings, and table-path preprocessing is restricted to bound owners.
3. **Hidden style/structure changes or resource leakage:** complete mixed-plan classification rejects grid/style/span/topology/action mutations; combined notes/table caps, complete change-limit failure, invalid-character retries, stale plans and semantic undo verify the existing lifecycle boundary.
4. **Unknown-content or cross-package data loss:** exact XML expectations, unselected payload hashes, graph comparisons, mixed-owner three-round saves, unlike source encodings and actual-output reprojection verify the candidate. No table reconstruction or resource deletion occurs.
5. **Conflating text retention and native fidelity:** producer geometry/representation/unknown-content changes are explicit. Native interactive and complete inherited gates remain open, as do direct styles/metrics, structural table operations, chart data, SmartArt/media/animation and the full active backlog.

### Final verification

`pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain exited **0**. The full run reports Parser **479**, Core **71**, Layout **126**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, browser bundles **29**, visual/measurement **27**, import integration **36**, release **16**, installed-package **5**, fixtures/performance **12**, composition **10**, copy **3**, chart parity **1**, CLI **1** and dev-app **16**, with zero failed/skipped tests. Earlier focused owner/table compatibility, browser and installed-package runs passed separately.

No full or inherited checkbox is closed by these character-only results.

## Node atomic file output (2026-10-25)

Concrete implementation progress on **IMP-09.5.3**. The new `@pptkit/pptx-parser/node` entry adds stable regular-file import and explicit local publication while the Parser root and browser bundles remain filesystem-neutral. [ADR 0011](../architecture/decisions/0011-node-file-publication.md) and the [public file contract](../api/file-save.md) define ownership and failure semantics. This bounded transaction slice does not close IMP-09.5.3's complete cross-process/platform matrix or any inherited rebuild, SmartArt, media or animation task.

### Implementation

`importPptxFile` captures import options before I/O, canonicalizes the parent, rejects symlink/non-regular sources, reads at most 64 KiB per chunk and verifies name/descriptor identity and complete bytes. Its receipt is a frozen nominal `{ path, byteLength, sha256 }`; native identity remains in an adapter-private WeakMap, and JSON serialization fails. `writePptxFile` requires an explicit `create` or `replace` union. Replace requires a receipt from the adapter for the same canonical path; create rejects any existing name.

Create publishes a flushed and independently readback-verified same-directory temporary inode through an exclusive hard link, then removes the temporary name. Replace verifies parent/path/regular-file identity, metadata and complete SHA256 before and after temporary construction, preserves ordinary `0777` bits, and publishes by same-directory atomic rename. Both modes sync file then directory, use process-local path/source-inode busy guards and return immutable receipts/warnings.

The runtime-neutral `session.assertSavePlanCurrent()` shares the private `save()` ownership, liveness, consent and full normalized-freshness implementation without copying bytes. Node calls it after the last asynchronous work immediately before the system link/rename dispatch. Session edits/disposal and plan disposal at that boundary reject before publication instead of relying on an earlier `save()` result.

Precommit failure preserves the original, cleans handles/temporary names where possible and retains a retryable session/plan. Precommit cleanup failure is `file-cleanup-failed`, explicitly uncommitted, with any leftover temporary path. Postcommit cancellation cannot roll back; directory sync, close and temporary unlink failures return a committed receipt with `directorySynced: false` where applicable and immutable warning codes. Stable Node provides no cross-process compare-and-swap between final verification and rename, so the contract assumes cooperating writers and a trusted/stable parent; a hostile external final-interval writer, Windows/native filesystem certification, owner/ACL/xattr/alternate-stream preservation and remote-filesystem identity behavior remain open.

### Tests and evidence

Added **19 test groups**: 17 [focused Parser cases](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/file-save.test.mjs) and two [real mixed-deck integration cases](../../scripts/file-save-parity.test.mjs). Parser coverage independently injects each parent/temp open, write, file sync/close, readback/hash, parent/temp verification, link and rename failure; exercises short/zero/corrupt writes, cancellation timing, source growth/shrink/replacement, target content/inode/mode/symlink/parent changes, final-boundary edits/disposal, same-process contention, independent-adapter create races, permissive mode retention, postcommit warnings, cleanup failure, bounds and retry. Integration performs three in-place mixed-table saves, compares file output with byte-session candidates, hashes unselected ZIP payloads independently and requires no temporary/baseline drift. Browser tests keep unchanged count **29** while checking root/browser isolation and the new synchronous guard; packed installed Node/type consumers exercise public `/node` imports and branded/frozen contracts.

The [retained mixed-deck evidence](../testing/file-save-evidence.json) uses the validated `text-runs-v3` source from the earlier table experiment. An exclusive creation and receipt-guarded replacement preserve the source hash exactly, retain mode `0640`, sync both directories, emit no warning and leave no temporary name. A later permission mutation causes the old receipt to reject as `file-target-changed`. This is local macOS filesystem evidence, not a second-process or native-application pass.

### Adversarial review

1. **Mutation after candidate preparation:** a synchronous final normalized/ownership/liveness/consent guard follows the last await and runs without a JavaScript gap before publication syscall dispatch. A lower-level asynchronous filesystem call may itself yield internally, but no caller code runs between the guard and the call expression. Session/plan mutation tests verify rejection.
2. **Overwrite authority confused with visible receipt fields:** snapshots are adapter-instance private and nominal; copied/forged/cross-adapter values reject. Path, device/inode, size, mode, mtime and full content are checked, including an equal-byte new inode and masked-mtime conflict. Standard Node lacks cross-process CAS after the final check; the remaining rename race is documented rather than misrepresented as universal atomic overwrite.
3. **Clobber or partial publication:** create uses exclusive hard link with no check-then-overwrite, and replace uses same-parent rename after full temporary readback/hash and target revalidation. Every precommit stage was independently fault-injected; candidates are bounded by the existing plan and 64 KiB chunk contracts.
4. **Misreporting a committed file as failed:** cancellation/temp cleanup/directory sync/close failures after dispatch return a successful reusable receipt with warnings, because rollback is no longer possible. Precommit cleanup failures retain `committed: false` and surface a leftover path.
5. **Browser/application overreach:** Parser root/browser exports contain no Node APIs, installed consumers verify `/node`, and no application compatibility, ACL/xattr fidelity, Windows guarantee or cross-process transaction is claimed. The full backlog remains active.

### Verification

`pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and the complete `pnpm test` chain exited **0**. The final full run reports Parser **496**, Core **71**, Layout **126**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, browser bundles **29**, visual/measurement **27**, import integration **38**, release **16**, installed-package **5**, fixtures/performance **12**, composition **10**, copy **3**, chart parity **1**, CLI **1** and dev-app **16**, with zero failed/skipped tests. Logs: `.tmp/file-save-full-build.log`, `.tmp/file-save-full-typecheck.log`, `.tmp/file-save-full-lint.log`, `.tmp/file-save-full-test.log`.

The full-backlog objective remains active. Next source-save work continues with object/property/topology preservation, structural reconciliation, loss-bearing rebuild, SmartArt, media and animation, while IMP-09.5.3 still needs a separately designed cross-process transaction protocol and broader filesystem/native certification.


## Page Fade transition semantics (2026-10-25)

Implementation progress on **ADV-05.1.1**, whose native interactive/settings/playback gates remain open. [ADR 0012](../architecture/decisions/0012-slide-fade-transition.md), the [API contract](../api/slide-transitions.md) and its checked example define the exact admitted surface.

Core adds an optional immutable Fade snapshot, replacement/clear and independently copied page values. Duration and automatic advance use unsigned integer milliseconds, including zero; click defaults to true, and manual action-only navigation remains legal. Normalized IR and Layout transport detached values without clocks or playback state; absent transition fields preserve earlier IR shapes. Static SVG is exactly unchanged by removing transitions. Custom slide implementations must implement the additive getter/setter.

Exporter writes native page-level Fade, exact `p14:dur` with local MCE ignorable declaration and a documented coarse speed fallback. Advance-from-page and transition-into-page are separate values. Custom PPTKit slide metadata excludes the transition field. Parser reads the selected transition's verified raw origin without globally admitting all Office2010 extension branches. Both projections map plain Fade with exact duration; speed-only, through-black, sounds and unknown markup remain source-only. Transition mutation blocks current preservation saves; unrelated bound text changes retain the original transition XML.

Added **nine test groups**: two [Core groups](../../packages/core/test/transitions.test.mjs), four [Parser groups](../../packages/pptx-parser/test/transitions.test.mjs) and three [integration groups](../../scripts/transition-parity.test.mjs). Evidence covers immutable snapshots/copy/clear/defaults and failed capture, located malformed/boundary values, both dialects and projections, exact no-op, stale plans, ignored raw unknown attributes/children, inactive MCE duration isolation, three fresh rounds, detached Layout, static SVG equality, exact unknown/known XML retention during unrelated edits, independent Chromium DOM timing and public browser/Node agreement. Installed Node/type consumers also exercise authoring and import through actual package exports.

Adversarial review:

1. **Arbitrary duration silently quantized:** the initial draft only serialized three speeds. It now writes exact `p14:dur`; independent DOM checks require 1234 ms and three-round imports retain it. Speed fallback is explicitly approximate. Zero/maximum integer boundaries and fractional/nonfinite values have direct tests.
2. **MCE or unknown content gaining permission:** raw children/attributes participate in admission, so ignored unknown extensions cannot disappear behind an effective Fade. A Fallback never borrows duration from an inactive Choice. Unknown/source-only transitions remain byte-preserved during no-op and unrelated text saves.
3. **Inconsistent state or hidden timers:** snapshots detach caller values, failed capture leaves prior state, duplicates are independent, and Layout cannot mutate Core. Static SVG equality proves that transition metadata introduces no hidden playback or first-frame visibility change.
4. **Unsupported source edits or metadata leakage:** whole-document preservation analysis blocks transition changes. Transition serialization is native page markup only and is excluded from custom metadata; source baseline/freshness and existing candidate reprojection apply.
5. **Overstating native evidence:** automated DOM/ZIP/Core checks establish the named data profile only. Native editable settings, slideshow playback/repair behavior, other effects and preview playback remain untested/open. ADV-05.1.1 and its parents stay unchecked; the full-backlog goal remains active.


Final verification: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and complete `pnpm test` exited **0**. Logs are `.tmp/fade-build.log`, `.tmp/fade-typecheck.log`, `.tmp/fade-lint.log` and `.tmp/fade-full-test.log`. The full run reports Core **73**, Parser **500**, Layout **126**, Exporter **49**, SVG **19**, standalone browser bundles **29**, visual/measurement **27**, import integration **41**, release **16**, installed-package **5**, fixtures/performance **12**, composition **10**, copy **3**, chart parity **1**, CLI **1** and dev-app **16**, with zero failed/skipped cases. The transition integration includes an additional independent Chromium DOM case within the 41 import groups.


## Native Fade timing experiment (2026-10-25)

The previous turn made implementation progress; this turn adds concrete native evidence under ADV-05.1.1. The [optional runner](../../scripts/inspect-slide-transitions.mjs) generates three named Fade inputs, verifies public original-byte no-op, uses an isolated LibreOfficeDev profile to save and reopen the saved PPTX to PDF, and inspects all source/native transition records through independent Python ZIP/ElementTree. Hashes, exact application/platform and command logs live in [the evidence](../testing/slide-transition-evidence.json).

LibreOfficeDev 26.8.0.0.alpha0 retains 1234 and 2000 ms duration, changes zero duration to 10 ms, rounds/truncates 3456 ms advance to 3000 ms and removes disabled-click policies. It emits exact-duration Choice and speed-only Fallback. Current Parser deliberately selects the Fallback and reports source-only rather than borrowing inactive duration. Source exact-byte preservation passes. Successful native reopen is distinct from interactive settings, repair-dialog absence, slideshow timing, visual fidelity and PowerPoint; all remain untested.

Adversarial review checked three failure modes: native conversion success hiding changed timing (record per-case duration/click/advance comparisons); shared Parser/writer errors hiding source faults (independent Python attributes assert the expected source values); and selected Fallback falsely acquiring inactive Choice milliseconds (native inventory and current Parser statuses remain separate). These observations keep ADV-05.1.1 unchecked. No production capability expands in this experiment; the full objective remains active.


Verification for the native experiment: runner executed successfully twice with fresh isolated profiles, final output `.tmp/fade-native.log`; `pnpm lint` passed including structure, boundaries, documentation links and checked examples (`.tmp/fade-native-lint.log`). Runner syntax and `git diff --check` pass. A separate Python hash audit matches retained source/native/reopened-PDF artifacts against the recorded SHA256 values. ## Cardinal Push/Wipe transitions (2026-10-25)

Advances **ADV-05.1.2** with all eight directional data variants. Core's existing transition snapshot becomes a discriminated union: Fade has no direction; Push/Wipe require left/right/up/down. Whole-document validation rejects missing/unknown directions and accidental Fade direction fields. Exporter writes native p:push/p:wipe direction letters while retaining exact duration/advance semantics and coarse speed fallback. Parser validates the selected raw effect, requires explicit direction and refuses unknown raw attributes/children; missing direction remains source-only. No global extension namespace capability or playback state is added.

Three new package groups (one Core, two Parser) cover all variants, both dialects, direction errors and raw ignored-attribute/subtree counterexamples. An additional integration group checks all eight directions over three fresh rounds, exact no-op, independent Chromium DOM attributes and unchanged static SVG. Installed Node/type consumers exercise public direction fields and mandatory typing. These extend the prior Fade suite; original Fade behavior and native evidence remain separately scoped.

Adversarial review checks: direction means movement/reveal rather than starting edge (explicit native-letter mapping with independent DOM expected values); absent native direction gains no guessed Core value; ignored opaque attributes/subtrees do not gain mapping through MCE; earlier Fade input remains compatible and rejects unrelated direction properties; static SVG remains final-state and does not imply playback/interruption support. Native Push/Wipe settings/slideshow and interruption remain untested. The complete task and full goal remain active.

## Native side-transition evidence (2026-10-25)

Concrete progress on ADV-05.1.2 application evidence. The optional transition runner now accepts `--all`, preserving its default three-Fade workflow and evidence file. Eleven slides cover three Fade controls and all eight Push/Wipe cardinal combinations. LibreOfficeDev retains each 765 ms side duration but changes every 2345 ms advance to 2000 ms and removes click-disabled policies. Push left/right letters are reversed in saved XML; up/down and all Wipe letters stay equal. Parser still chooses speed-only Fallback and exposes source-only status. Actual native movement, editable settings, interruption, PowerPoint and preview playback remain open, and the full goal remains active.

## Finite motion timing research (2026-10-25)

Progress on ADV-06.1.1 through [ADR 0013](../architecture/decisions/0013-finite-motion-time-research.md) and a private, executable tooling prototype. No production package/public API is added. Integer-millisecond finite trees define sequence/parallel span, parent/child delays, integer repetition, abstract hold/remove end state and random-access progress. The prototype rejects unknown node/properties, negative/fractional/nonfinite times, unsafe arithmetic, cycles and duplicate IDs, and enforces visited-node/depth/time budgets before expansion. Output is detached/frozen; no clock or playhead writes into Core/Layout.

Five research groups verify manually calculated nested timestamps, same-time zero-duration ordering, exact start/repeat/end boundaries and seek-order independence, snapshot/freeze, malformed/opaque/accessor/cycle/overflow rejection, budget retry and 200 seeded trees against a separate relative-interval oracle. The test command is integrated into root `pnpm test` to retain reproducible evidence.

Adversarial review: zero-duration/repeat boundaries avoid division by zero and define end exactly; combined parent/child and repeated time arithmetic cannot overflow silently; unknown or infinite nodes never become fabricated finite waits; source mutation and failed budget attempts cannot change existing plans; abstract progress/event lists are not presented as visible effects or native/event playback. Fractional/infinite repeat, auto-reverse, easing, group repetition, event/target identities and composition remain open. The full goal and ADV-06.1.1 stay active/incomplete.

## Explicit cached SmartArt drawing preview (2026-10-25)

Partial ADV-01.1.2 through [ADR0018](../architecture/decisions/0018-cached-diagram-preview.md) and the [content option contract](../api/diagram-preview.md). Content-only `diagramPreview: "cache"` projects associated DSP drawing shapes into ordinary Core groups, with frame/root/nested coordinate spaces, seven presets, direct text/paint/stroke and bounded rectangular raster fills. Default import remains unchanged. Cached drawing source paths, unknown freshness and cache/partial-cache/placeholder records stay Parser-owned; no data-node model or native editability is inferred.

Unavailable/empty/MCE caches receive explicit placeholders; unsupported geometry/text transforms and properties are located fallback findings. Malformed mapped scalar fields fail with cache-part provenance. The normal image reader is reused through an explicit fill input, preserving shared asset identity and aggregate byte limits. External images are not fetched. A dedicated aggregate preview-work budget charges repeated shared-cache traversal. Cached text is deliberately unbound; cache mutations block preservation saves while unrelated supported text edits use actual-candidate reprojection with the same captured option. The Node file adapter captures and forwards the selection before IO. Direct fresh output is verified to contain ordinary shapes, never native SmartArt.

Eight new Parser groups cover explicit/default/invalid options and mutation-after-call capture, both dialects, root/nested origins and transforms, unavailable/empty/MCE/wrong-root caches, hidden/unknown geometry, image sharing/external/budgets, cache-part failures, source-save boundaries and Node propagation. A single cache fits a 250-checkpoint budget while twenty shared uses reject, proving aggregate charging. Two import-integration groups run public browser bundles without network and independently check visible rectangle/image bounds (110/70/100/50 and 280/70/100/50), text visibility, Node/browser report/render parity, and three unrelated save rounds including mapped cache-image assets. Screenshots are retained at `.tmp/diagram-preview/transitional.png` and `.tmp/diagram-preview/strict.png`; visual inspection confirms the expected synthetic geometry/text/image output. These are not native rendering comparisons.

The [four-source experiment](../testing/smartart-inventory-evidence.json) now supports `--preview`. Independent Python raw XML confirms that all three located source caches have zero visual children and cycle2 has no recognized cache reference. All four correctly produce unavailable placeholders; generated source-probe SVGs remain in the retained corpus directory. This prevents treating a located empty part as a visible native cache. Nonempty native cache fidelity, stale-cache policy, complete themes/geometry/text transforms, source fixture licensing admission and native node editing remain open; neither ADV-01.1.2 nor the overall implementation goal is closed.

Adversarial review checked cached shapes being mistaken for editable native nodes, lost preview options during Node import/save validation, shared-cache work amplification, group-origin/scaling errors and empty/unknown content silently becoming a blank successful preview. Source binding guards, consistent candidate reprojection, dedicated work charging, hand-specified geometry checks, visible placeholders and independent raw source counts provide the current evidence. Unsupported native semantics remain explicit.

Unavailable text is classified before rich-text parsing; a flipped transform with an invalid text size proves that the opaque body is not spuriously validated. Identity wrappers, located fallback reports, independent source/browser geometry and existing unbound-edit guards cover the other risks. Flip/upright semantics, full fonts/paints/custom geometry, cache freshness and native SmartArt editing remain open; the overall implementation objective remains incomplete.

No native application was launched and source results do not establish native editing, re-layout or visual fidelity.

Adversarial review checked five likely regressions: numeric/cross-kind identity collisions, treating repeated cache references as data duplicates, multiplying shared data output, losing custom limits at file/candidate boundaries, and exposing unverified graph/layout semantics as editable capabilities. Explicit identity/ref statuses, deduplicated reports, the above-default real-file regression, independent source/DOM expectations and source-only/MCE boundaries provide scoped evidence. ## Explicit SmartArt source mappings (2026-10-25)

Progress on ADV-02.1.2.2 through [ADR0021](../architecture/decisions/0021-smartart-explicit-model-mapping.md) and the [API contract](../api/diagram-mapping.md). Presentation mappings retain direct prSet association and incoming presOf connection evidence once per data part, with raw point/connection indices and descriptive none/same-target/different-targets/unresolved agreement. Cache mappings resolve recognized DSP tree shape model IDs against the owning data part, once per explicitly associated pair. Broad-kind IDs distinguish absent/unrecognized/missing/ambiguous/wrong-kind/resolved targets; duplicate visual references remain legitimate separate records. No text owner, transitive semantic model or editable Core diagram is inferred.

Only direct recognized shape-tree/group paths participate. Raw extension/inactive cache shape inventory stays separate; MCE caches, absent/duplicate trees and source-only data produce source-only pair reports rather than invented empty graphs. An explicit empty cache tree can have an inspected zero-shape reference pass without implying visible content. Actual cache associations determine pairs, never filename, nearby parts or a resource Cartesian product. Shared owners, same data with different slide-local caches and distinct data identity spaces using one cache retain correct resolution scopes.

Graph work now includes normalized indices/evidence, recognized cache scans, owner traversal and actual pair resolution; mapping findings share the existing cumulative diagnostic budget. Model indices and cache scans are reused rather than copied for every owner. Existing diagram option snapshots survive byte/file capture and actual text-save candidate reprojection. Original no-op bytes and three unrelated text-save rounds preserve graph payloads and mappings, without adding diagram-edit bindings.

Eight new focused Parser groups cover both dialects, one-to-many and repeated shapes, differing/unresolved/no association evidence, identity aliases and cross-kind duplicates, nested groups versus extension wrappers, MCE/tree/data source-only boundaries, shared and unassigned pairs, aggregate budgets, detached output and no-op. The existing independent browser DOM group now derives presentation evidence/cache targets directly from XML and compares Node/browser public reports; the three-round source-save group compares mappings as well as payloads. Installed Node, browser ESM/global and readonly type consumers assert the new types and paths. A focused run across graph/resource/mapping/browser/file suites passed 33 groups.

The independent Python ZIP/ElementTree source experiment verifies 103 presentation mappings over [four original sources](../testing/smartart-mapping-evidence.json) and [three further sources](../testing/smartart-mapping-text-evidence.json). process3 has two differing-target presentation nodes: the report retains those explicit facts without declaring corruption or choosing a text owner. Three nonempty caches resolve ten shapes, while original controls retain three empty trees and one missing cache. Exact no-op, all prior source graph invariants and seven independent text frames remain checked. No native application was launched; licensing admission for redistribution of the local third-party inputs remains pending.

Adversarial review checked five likely failures: pairing an unassigned cache with data, crossing slide/data identity scopes, suppressing multiple-source evidence, interpreting extension shapes as effective children, and amplification from shared owners or unlimited mapping findings. Explicit pair deduplication, per-data identity indices, full evidence arrays, recognized-path/MCE guards and aggregate budget tests cover those risks. Mapping success remains source evidence, without freshness, native rendering/editability or family layout approval. ## Scoped SmartArt source-to-managed bindings — 2026-10-25

Progress on ADV-02.2.1.2.1 and ADV-02.1.2.4 through [ADR0027](../architecture/decisions/0027-smartart-source-binding-research.md), [source-binding adapter](../../scripts/research/diagram-source-bindings.mjs) and [protocol](../testing/smartart-source-bindings.md). Source model IDs, managed candidate IDs and emitted native IDs remain distinct. The adapter captures caller scope, exact part/layout, canonical virtual-root ID, active source points and reserved managed IDs. Reinspection preserves identities across raw XML index/storage/order/text changes. Signed int32 spellings normalize under the existing Parser profile while original source spelling stays in bindings. Uppercase braced GUIDs remain explicit. Context/root mismatch, canonical collisions, unknown/partial graphs and any graph findings reject; source scope is not inferred from labels or geometry.

Observed retirement leaves a reservation but removes the active source ID. Returning retired source IDs require an exact explicit restore list; unknown/active/root/duplicate restore entries reject. Fresh source IDs allocate fresh managed IDs. The adapter is pure and detached; failed allocation/budgets leave the previous snapshot intact. The unchanged content projection now has a separate budgeted entry point so projection, validation, remapping and identity reconciliation share one maxWork. Context/raw/canonical/reservation/restore bytes share a cumulative key budget. Individual raw identities have a bounded lexical profile. Caller source lineage, distinct namespaces and serialized state commits remain explicit responsibilities; no authenticated baseline, shared-data instance reconciliation or concurrent-fork merge is claimed.

Seven new pure groups include canonical lexical/collision controls, source context/root guards, retirement/restoration, corruption, exact work/key thresholds, failure/retry, hierarchy roles, Cycle closure and a hundred seeded reinspection rounds with identical labels checked against an independent allocation oracle. The existing seven content groups remain unchanged. A new native-research integration group covers three families × two caches × integer/GUID source identities: browser DOM moves point records and changes lexical IDs/raw indices and root order; actual Parser output binds to stable managed IDs, then two fresh stateful generations retain native mappings. The first control run exposed DOM XMLSerializer emitting CR references as literal CR; the controlled mutation now re-escapes CR so subsequent XML newline normalization does not alter text accidentally. The corrected integration passes.

The optional [real-file verifier](../../scripts/inspect-smartart-source-bindings.mjs) produces [five groups/eleven versions](../testing/smartart-source-bindings-evidence.json) from existing hash-pinned inputs and retained native outputs. Independent Python ZIP/XML reconstructs parent order, plain paragraphs, roles, raw source indices, canonical identities, monotonic allocation and all reservations. Process retains four managed IDs and adds one; Hierarchy retains five and adds one; the GUID Cycle input plus three native text rounds retain three; Cycle native deletion retains four and removes one. A separate integer-input/native-saved pair is an expected rejection: Python independently proves the root identity changed and the adapter refuses context continuity. No new native UI operation was performed or replayed, and the stateful fresh writer does not inherit native admission from these source checks. Third-party artifacts remain local.

Adversarial review addressed five likely failures: (1) using source traversal IDs or equal labels as identity, prevented by raw canonical bindings and identical-label seeded tests; (2) silently recycling an observed retired source ID, prevented by reservations and exact restore guards; (3) forcing producer-renumbered graphs together, prevented by context/root rejection and an independently proved mismatch case; (4) constructing managed content from incomplete source text/topology, prevented by graph-finding rejection plus existing field/topology validation; (5) resetting budgets or committing state during failed preparation, prevented by shared counters, exact boundary tests and detached results. Unobserved delete/reuse of an active source ID cannot be detected from snapshots alone and remains an explicit lineage limitation.

Validation: `pnpm build`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test` passed; the full suite includes 26 diagram research and 65 import groups plus all existing package/visual/browser/release/consumer checks. After the final graph-finding rejection guard, all 22 current binding/native research groups passed again. The optional eleven-version verifier and independent root-mismatch rejection passed.
